# Midtown Madness 2 progress

This is the durable handoff log for the MM2 agent loop. Each iteration should make one focused change, run a targeted check, and append a short entry here.

## Baseline

- Local input: `game/Midtown2.exe` and `game/MIDTOWN2.ICD` (ignored, user-provided)
- Format: PE32 Intel 80386 GUI executables
- Primary input: `game/Midtown2.exe`, the cracked/unpacked PE usable by Theseus
- `MIDTOWN2.ICD` is the protected original image with scrambled on-disk code
- Translation input: `out/mm2/translate.sh` defaults to the cracked executable; `MM2_INPUT` selects an alternate usable PE
- ICD reconstruction: optional comparison path, not a blocker for the current port
- Compiler baseline: the launcher scan found 99,382 blocks and about 93.6% code-section coverage
- Compiler blocker: `Float80` handling in `tc/src/codegen/mod.rs::mem_size` and x87 load/store codegen is resolved by the latest iteration
- Other compiler findings: two statically known jump targets were not discovered

## Ordered backlog

- [x] Identify and use the cracked `game/Midtown2.exe` as the reproducible PE source.
- [ ] Optionally reconstruct a decrypted ICD image for comparison with the protected release.
- [ ] Add an input validation/reconstruction tool if the optional ICD path becomes necessary.
- [x] Define and test correct handling for x87 `Float80` memory operands.
- [x] Regenerate the cracked MM2 snapshot and fix the initial compiler/code-generation failures.
- [x] Compile the generated `mm2` crate and resolve the startup Win32/DirectX exports.
- [x] Run the translated program from `game/` and verify no dynamic missing addresses.
- [x] Establish stable menu, lobby, quit, and race runtime milestones.
- [ ] Add and test selected Rust function overrides.
- [x] Document a repeatable macOS run/debug workflow after the first successful launch.

## Iteration log

- Initial assessment: renamed the local installation to `game/`, added the root ignore rule, created the `out/mm2` target scaffold, and confirmed the translator reaches static discovery before failing on `Float80` code generation.
- Loader assessment: `Midtown2.exe` is a small loader and `MIDTOWN2.ICD` is a PE32 image with scrambled on-disk code. The target now requires a private decrypted/reconstructed ICD image through `MM2_INPUT`; `tc` accepts `.icd` inputs for that workflow.
- Float80 iteration: `MM2_INPUT=game/Midtown2.exe out/mm2/translate.sh` initially reproduced the `Float80` panic at `tc/src/codegen/mod.rs:190`; after adding 10-byte x87 extended-memory conversion and load/store codegen, the same command completed with 99,382 blocks. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check runtime/src/fpu.rs runtime/src/lib.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs tc/src/codegen/fpu.rs && cargo check -p runtime -p tc && cargo test -p runtime -p tc` passed (10 tests). `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` reached generated-crate compilation but failed on missing Win32 stdcall exports and unresolved `imm32`/`dplayx` modules. Next blocker: inventory and implement the missing generated Win32/DirectX imports needed for the `mm2` crate to compile.
- GetSystemDefaultLangID iteration: `cargo fmt --all -- --check` failed because the unrelated `out/winpin/src/generated.rs` module is absent; `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/kernel32/nls.rs && cargo check -p winapi && cargo test -p winapi` passed (1 test). Added `kernel32::GetSystemDefaultLangID`, returning the US-English `LANGID` expected by the generated startup code. `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` then failed with 232 errors (down from 234), with `user32::EnumDisplaySettingsA_stdcall` the first remaining missing export. Next blocker: implement `user32::EnumDisplaySettingsA` with the existing display-mode model rather than stubbing the generated call.
- EnumDisplaySettingsA iteration: `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/user32/misc.rs && cargo check -p winapi && cargo test -p winapi` passed (1 test and zero doc-tests). Implemented `user32::EnumDisplaySettingsA` for current and registry settings, validating `DEVMODE.dmSize` and writing the existing 640x480x32/60Hz display mode. `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` failed with 230 errors (down from 232); the first remaining missing export is `shell32::ShellExecuteA_stdcall`. Next blocker: implement `shell32::ShellExecuteA` with behavior appropriate for the translated startup path.
- ShellExecuteA iteration: `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/shell32.rs && cargo check -p winapi && cargo test -p winapi` passed (1 test and zero doc-tests). Added `shell32::ShellExecuteA` with null-file failure reporting and an explicit no-association result for unsupported host shell execution, allowing the translated startup path to continue without a broad stub. `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` still failed; `ShellExecuteA_stdcall` is resolved and `user32::ChangeDisplaySettingsA_stdcall` is now the first remaining missing export. Next blocker: implement `user32::ChangeDisplaySettingsA` against the existing display-mode model.

- Divide-error iteration: an operand probe confirmed `div`/`idiv` expose only the divisor (`op_count() == 1`) while the dividend is the implicit AX/DX:AX/EDX:EAX pair, which the existing lowering already reads correctly. Generated code now checks `divisor == 0`, uses `checked_div`/`checked_rem` for signed overflow, and range-checks the quotient before packing results. - FFREE/tag-model iteration: `FFREE` marks a register empty without moving TOP, which the positional stack model could not represent, so the FPU gained a per-register `tags` array. `push`/`pop` mark and clear tags, `tag_word`, `abridged_tag`, and `load_status` (including `fxrstor`) consume or restore them, and `init` clears them. Because data pops tag the freed slot while `FINCSTP` must leave tags untouched, `Fincstp` now lowers to a new `inc_top` instead of `pop`; the existing constants/stack-ops regression was updated to pin that. `rustfmt --edition 2024 runtime/src/fpu.rs tc/src/codegen/fpu.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs && rustfmt --edition 2024 --check runtime/src/fpu.rs tc/src/codegen/fpu.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs && cargo test -p runtime -p tc` passed with 92 runtime tests, 77 compiler tests, and zero doc-test failures; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` and `THESEUS_MISSING_ADDRS=out/mm2/missing.txt cargo run --profile fast -p mm2 -- game` passed with six generated self-assignment warnings, exit 0, and no `missing.txt`. Because they are privileged in Windows usermode, `check_instr` now bails on `In`/`Ins`/`Outs` alongside `Out` for non-DOS modules, keeping the gather's decode-garbage policy consistent. `rustfmt --edition 2024 runtime/src/ops/string.rs tc/src/codegen/string.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs tc/src/gather/block.rs && rustfmt --edition 2024 --check runtime/src/ops/string.rs tc/src/codegen/string.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs tc/src/gather/block.rs && cargo test -p runtime -p tc` passed with 94 runtime tests, 79 compiler tests, and zero doc-test failures. - GetDeviceCaps iteration: the stale generated snapshot's remaining panic strings (`Fnsave`/`Frstor`, `In`, `Int1`/`Int3`, `Jo`/`Jno`/`Jp`/`Jnp`, `Sal`, `les`, `SegPtr32`, `int 0x6`) are all covered by the tracked compiler, so the audit moved to shared winapi `todo!()`s reachable from the generated export table. `gdi32::GetDeviceCaps` is imported by the target and previously panicked for every index except `NUMCOLORS`. - user32 handle iteration: `LoadCursor`/`LoadIcon` (A and W) now return stable opaque handles keyed by (instance, name) from a `0xD000_0000`+ counter, `CreateCursor` allocates one, `SetCursor` records and returns the previous handle, `ShowCursor` keeps the real display counter, `DestroyWindow` drops the window and clears the message queue's reference, `RegisterClass` returns a real class atom from `0xC000`+, and `CreateWindowEx*` returns its HWND directly. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` and `cargo check -p winapi` and `cargo test -p winapi` (46 tests) and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. - kernel32/winmm iteration: `GetEnvironmentVariableA` returned the environ-block pointer where the API contract is a copied-length-or-0 — fixed to 0 since the emulated environment is empty; `GetEnvironmentStringsW`/`FreeEnvironmentStrings*` are deliberate returns, not stubs, so their `stub!` markers are gone; `GetModuleFileNameA` writes the executable name taken from the first token of the process command line; `mciSendCommandA`'s no-op success is deliberate too — the `stub!` marker became a `log::debug` that records the message id. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` and `cargo check -p winapi` and `cargo test -p winapi` (46 tests) and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: the advapi32 `Reg*` stubs — `RegOpenKeyExA` "succeeded" without writing `*phkResult`, leaving the caller a garbage HKEY.
- Registry iteration: `advapi32` now carries a minimal in-memory registry: `RegCreateKeyExW` records keys and hands out handles with real NEW/EXISTING dispositions, `RegOpenKeyExA` resolves only existing keys (`ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` on a fresh registry, `ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE` on unknown bases), `RegQueryValueExA/W` report stored values with the documented size-query and `ERROR_MORE_DATA` paths, `RegSetValueExW` stores typed values, and `RegCloseKey` validates handles. Previously `RegOpenKeyExA` returned success without writing `*phkResult`, so the caller's HKEY was uninitialized. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` and `cargo check -p winapi` and `cargo test -p winapi` (46 tests) and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: final headless verification of the menu and clean exit.
- Warning-hygiene iteration: `UpdateWindow` marks the window dirty (the queue already turns that into WM_PAINT on the next pump), and `CreateDirectoryA` no longer warns on `AlreadyExists` — the game probe-creates its `players\` dirs every launch. Zero `stub:` markers fire; the earlier ~30 `SelectObject` null warnings are gone. A full headless run with `THESEUS_INJECT_VKEY=0x1b,0x0d` at 25s exited cleanly with status 0 and `/tmp/mm2final.ppm` shows the menu rendered at 640×480. Remaining blockers are unchanged: Direct3D rasterizer, DirectPlay lobby, MSVFW32 movies, `nodeGetBitmap` art misses, and the absent decrypted image.

The `CreateFileA` parent-directory behavior is correct shared Win32 handling and may help other on-demand file creation. Next blocker: the remaining `LoadLibrary(MSVFW32.DLL)` and `LoadLibrary(VTUNEAPI.DLL)` startup warnings, which are small independent Win32 gaps.

- Direct3D draw diagnostics: added `log::warn!` and `log_vertex_start` to `IDirect3DDevice7::DrawPrimitive`, `DrawIndexedPrimitive`, `DrawPrimitiveStrided`, `DrawIndexedPrimitiveStrided`, `DrawPrimitiveVB`, and `DrawIndexedPrimitiveVB` to log primitive type, FVF, vertex/index counts, and the first few vertex bytes. `rustfmt --edition 2024 win32/winapi/src/ddraw/d3d7.rs && cargo check -p winapi && cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. `RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 ... timeout 20 ./target/fast/mm2 game 2>&1 | tee /tmp/mm2d3d.log` showed that the race loop only uses `DrawIndexedPrimitive` and `DrawPrimitive` with `prim=4` (`D3DPT_TRIANGLESTRIP`) and `fvf=0x1c4` (`D3DFVF_XYZRHW | D3DFVF_DIFFUSE | D3DFVF_SPECULAR | D3DFVF_TEX1`, 32 bytes/vertex). - Minimal 16bpp Direct3D 7 rasterizer: `IDirect3DDevice7::DrawPrimitive` and `DrawIndexedPrimitive` now call a focused `rasterize` helper that handles `D3DPT_TRIANGLESTRIP` with `D3DFVF_XYZRHW | D3DFVF_DIFFUSE | D3DFVF_SPECULAR | D3DFVF_TEX1` (32 bytes/vertex) on the 640x480 RGB565 back buffer. It reads `RhwVertex` positions and texture coordinates, generates triangles from the strip, and fills each pixel with the sampled 16bpp texture or an interpolated diffuse color. `rustfmt --edition 2024 win32/winapi/src/ddraw/d3d7.rs && cargo check -p winapi && cargo test -p winapi && cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 THESEUS_FRAME_DUMP=/tmp/mm2render.ppm THESEUS_MISSING_ADDRS=out/mm2/missing.txt ... timeout 25 ./target/fast/mm2 game` rendered the `BLITZ London's Calling` loading/race screen instead of a black frame (see `/tmp/mm2render.png`). With `RUST_LOG=error` the run is much quieter and reaches the London level load; the rasterizer is the dominant CPU cost. - Rasterizer correctness iteration: the 16bpp rasterizer now alternates `D3DPT_TRIANGLESTRIP` triangle winding for every second triangle and uses perspective-correct texture interpolation (`u*w` and `v*w` divided by the interpolated `w`). `rustfmt --edition 2024 win32/winapi/src/ddraw/d3d7.rs && cargo check -p winapi && cargo test -p winapi && cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. `RUST_LOG=error ... timeout 60` reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 12.2M` but timed out before the game loop could present a clean frame; the final dump is a noisy/partial 3D image, indicating the 3D scene is being drawn but the per-pixel barycentric rasterizer is too slow and possibly still has texture-addressing issues. `RUST_LOG=error ... timeout 25` still reaches the `BLITZ London's Calling` UI and renders it correctly. Next blocker: speed up the rasterizer (edge-stepping, fixed-point or span-based) and diagnose the 3D frame noise.
- Texture-upload and dump-diagnostics iteration: `IDirect3DDevice7::Load` was a silent no-op while the game calls it constantly to move pixels between surfaces; it now validates both surface handles, resolves `lpDestPoint`/`lprcSrcRect` (`read_rect`/`blit_copy` shared helpers), and cascades through attached mip-level pairs with halved rects and points. Diagnostics added: `CreateSurface` now logs `dwFlags`/mipmap count/`lpSurface` (texture creates use `flags=0x21007`, mip chains come in as separate per-level surfaces), a `rasterize` warning fires once per bound texture whose `pixels` were never locked or blitted (zero observed — every bound texture had storage), `THESEUS_TEX_DUMP=<dir>` writes each bound 16bpp texture once as a PPM (dumps show real MM2 art, e.g. the Beetle body panels, so texture memory is now populated), and `THESEUS_FRAME_DUMP_EVERY=<n>` writes a numbered film strip instead of overwriting one file. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi`, `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. A 45s headless run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 12.2M` again; the frame sequence proves the title, menus, vehicle-select chrome, and the London loading screen all render correctly — the corruption is confined to the 3D rasterizer output (noise bands on the car model, noise frame in race). The game creates a real `DDSCAPS_ZBUFFER` 640x480x16 system-memory surface that the rasterizer never reads or writes, and `D3DRENDERSTATE_CULLMODE` is not honored, so strip junction triangles can draw across the screen. Next blocker: honor cull mode and wire the attached z-buffer into `rasterize` (depth test + `D3DCLEAR_ZBUFFER` handling).
- Cull-mode and z-buffer iteration: `rasterize` now reads `D3DRENDERSTATE_CULLMODE` (default `D3DCULL_CCW`), `ZENABLE`/`ZWRITEENABLE`/`ZFUNC` (defaults on/on/less-or-equal) and finds the `DDSCAPS_ZBUFFER` surface attached to the render target; `Clear` also handles `D3DCLEAR_ZBUFFER` by packing `dvZ` to the 16-bit attached depth surface. Per-pixel, z interpolates linearly in screen space and gates the write through `z_passes`. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi`, `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. A 180s headless run with `THESEUS_FRAME_DUMP_EVERY=30` produced 38 frames; the vehicle-select Beetle now renders cleanly (noise bands gone) and no frame matches the earlier all-noise md5, but the run still spends the entire window in level load (`AIMAP.Init`, LOD warnings) and ends at `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 12.2M` without an in-race present. Remaining run errors are external content gaps: missing `aud/aud22/*.22k` audio, `nodeGetBitmap` art misses, `london: room count mismatch`, `datParser` unknown tokens, and unimplemented DirectMusic (`DMusicObject::InitPerformance`). Next blocker: confirm the GameLoop actually presents (time-stamp the load) and speed up the per-pixel rasterizer or emulated CPU enough to see an in-race frame.
- x87 `fprem`/`fprem1` condition-code iteration: the generated code's reduction loop (`fprem` → `fnstsw ax` → `sahf` → `jp`) at `0x582ac6` spun forever because codegen computed only the remainder and never updated the FPU condition bits, leaving C2 set. Added `runtime::FPU::prem(nearest: bool)` which computes the remainder (`FPREM` truncating, `FPREM1` IEEE-nearest) and sets C0/C3/C1 from the low quotient bits while clearing C2 on completion; `tc/src/codegen/fpu.rs` now emits `ctx.cpu.fpu.prem(false/true)` instead of inline `%`/`round_ties_even` expressions, and the `tc` codegen test was updated to match. The stale generated snapshot was hand-patched at `out/mm2/src/generated/part_90.rs` (`ctx.cpu.fpu.prem(false)`) for local testing only; a fresh translation would emit the call directly. `cargo check -p runtime -p tc`, `cargo test -p runtime` (125 tests), `cargo test -p tc` (131 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. The headless run no longer hangs at the reduction loop and instead advanced to `jmp to null ptr` from a null COM interface. Next blocker: the null DirectMusic performance object.
- DirectMusic COM-object iteration: the post-`fprem` crash was a null indirect jump at generated `x5179e0` (`call dword ptr [edx+38h]`, `IDirectMusicPerformance2::IsPlaying`) because `ole32::CoCreateInstance` returned `REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG` for `CLSID_DirectMusicPerformance` (`{d2ac2881-b39b-11d1-8704-00600893b1bd}`), leaving the global at `0x6B0574` zero while the game calls it unconditionally every frame. Stubs are hand-rolled `ContFn`s so they can pop the exact stdcall arg count without needing argument names; out-params are zeroed where the contract demands, `IsPlaying` returns `FALSE`, `Init` hands back a real `IDirectMusic` object, and loader `GetObject`/`LoadObjectFromFile` fail cleanly so no segment objects are fabricated. `dplayx` helpers (`init_vtable`, `add_blocks`, `read_guid`, `IID_IUnknown`, `IID_NullUnknown`) were made `pub(crate)` for reuse, and `CoCreateInstance` now dispatches the two DirectMusic CLSIDs. `rustfmt --edition 2024` and `cargo check -p winapi` pass clean; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed with the six known warnings. `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 timeout 120 ./target/fast/mm2 game` ran the full window with no panic, and `THESEUS_FRAME_DUMP_EVERY=30` produced ~4,260 frames ending on a fully rendered `SELECT DRIVER` main menu (CRASH COURSE / RACES / MULTIPLAYER / QUICK RACE). Demoted all of them to `log::debug!` and made `log_vertex_start` early-out unless debug logging is enabled so it no longer reads guest memory per call. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/ddraw/d3d7.rs win32/winapi/src/ddraw/ddraw7.rs && cargo check -p winapi && cargo test -p winapi && cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed (46 winapi tests, six known generated self-assignment warnings). `RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 timeout 20 ./target/fast/mm2 game` is now readable: only honest warnings remain (missing `.CHK` checksums, `aud/aud22` `.22k` assets, `nodeGetBitmap` misses for `ulock_amvpcab`/`vpcab_desc`/`ama_rank_desc`). - Primitive-decomposition iteration: `rasterize` guarded on `dptPrimitiveType == 4` and then decomposed the stream as a triangle STRIP — but in D3D7 `D3DPT_TRIANGLELIST=4`, `TRIANGLESTRIP=5`, `TRIANGLEFAN=6`. So every in-race draw was either mis-decomposed (lists treated as strips) or dropped outright (the ~700 strip and ~7,600 fan calls). `rasterize` now expands the draw into an index stream and decomposes per type: lists take consecutive triples, strips keep the alternating winding, fans share `verts[0]`. Added a `rasterize:` debug line reporting `prim/verts/tris/wrote px/rt_surf/rt addr/size/tex/last color`, skip-reason debug logs on every early return, and a `Clear:`/`SetRenderTarget:` debug line. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi`, `cargo test -p winapi`, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. In-race the world is now rasterized into the render target — draws like `verts=265 tris=21 wrote 72208 px` and hundreds of textured `prim=6` fans write real varied colors (e.g. `color=0x7472`, `0x8b8c`).
- Flip/present investigation: the in-race pipeline is `Clear(color=0xffead6c6, the London fog beige)` → world+HUD rasterized into render-target surface `0x7e2468` (pixels `0x91246a`) → HUD `BltFast`s into `0x7e2468` → `BltFast dst=0x7e2460 src=0x7e2468` composites into the flip-chain back buffer → `Flip` presents `0x7e2460` (pixels `0x9a846e`). Two real fixes fell out of tracing this: `Surface::flip` only refreshed the back buffer's texture when the front surface had a palette, so in 16bpp mode every flip re-presented a stale texture — it now calls `update_texture` unconditionally. Diagnostics added: `flip:`/`Blt:`/`BltFast:` debug lines, `THESEUS_FLIP_DUMP`/`_AT` (dump the presented back buffer's raw pixels at the Nth flip), `THESEUS_SRC_DUMP`/`_AT` (dump the Nth 640x480 blit source). Both dumps prove the back buffer and the render-target source genuinely contain only fog-beige + HUD — so the world isn't lost in presentation: the game either submits no near-field geometry or everything is fully fogged. A `THESEUS_NO_ZTEST` run ruled out the z-buffer. Hand-patching generated `x444373` showed the room-count check compares `stored=1341` (file) vs `counted=1342` (the game's own count) — off by one, but rooms still load (`Room 337+ has Water of Death` prints after), so it is a version-warning, not a hard failure. The remaining theory for the blank world is that the car/camera is in the void: `Reset!!!` (the game's fall recovery) fires right after `AIMAP.Init`, and the down-pointing checkpoint arrow plus all-fog view match a car that fell through the level — i.e. a collision/physics problem, not a rasterizer one. Next blocker: confirm whether the car's world position is valid (trace the position/collision query the game uses for ground contact) and why the camera ends up below the map.
- World-visible iteration — the real cause: per frame the game issues `Clear(e1dcd7)` *before* `BeginScene`, rasterizes the whole world pass, then issues a second full-surface `Clear(ead6c6)` mid-scene before the HUD pass — so the world was drawn and then erased every frame. Both clears are `dwCount=0/lpRects=0` (the new rect-list honoring in `Clear` is implemented but unused here). `Device` now tracks `in_scene` (Begin/EndScene) and `drew_since_clear` (set when `rasterize` writes pixels); a Clear that arrives mid-scene after geometry landed now applies only the z-buffer portion and leaves the color target alone — the only reading consistent with the visible world. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check`, `cargo check -p winapi`, `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. `THESEUS_FRAME_DUMP_EVERY=200` now produces fully rendered in-race frames: the London street with buildings, sky, a white truck, the yellow New Beetle at the start line, the running HUD timer and minimap — the 3D world is visible for the first time. `Reset!!!` prints once after `AIMAP.Init` and the car sits at the start line, so it is a normal spawn reset, not a fall loop. Remaining content gaps are external (missing `aud/aud22` audio, `.CHK` checksums, `nodeGetBitmap` art, `london: room count mismatch` is a benign off-by-one). Next blocker: verify the race actually plays over a longer run (car movement, camera tracking, per-frame stability) and then audit audio/input paths.

(caller file:line)` pointing at the generated block or winapi call site. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi -p runtime`, `cargo test -p runtime -p winapi` (125 + 49 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed with zero warnings. It now returns an empty name for NULL, matching the API contract. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi`, `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. A 65s headless London Blitz run reached GameLoop with no `null page` error and no other new warnings; remaining output is missing-content (`aud22`, `.CHK`, `nodeGetBitmap`, LODs, `datParser` tokens) or the benign SelectObject diagnostic. - Roomcheck diagnosis iteration: a temporary `Memory::write` watchpoint (local only, reverted) showed the `ds:[628954h]` room counter is written exactly once per level load, at generated `x45c402` (`mov [ebx+4Ch], ecx`, `0x628908+0x4C`) — it is a struct field set from a caller-supplied total computed by the room parser, not a per-room increment that emulation could skew. `stored=1341` is the file header value and `counted=1342` is the parser's own tally, so the mismatch is a content version warning ("please re-import"), consistent with rooms loading, the world rendering, and collision working. The same run showed the `null page` error is gone and no new warnings appear. `cargo check -p runtime` clean after the revert. Remaining open items are all external: the absent decrypted PE (retranslation) and missing game content (`aud22` audio, `aud\dmusic` segments, `nodeGetBitmap` art, `.CHK` caches).
- Run/debug workflow iteration: documented the repeatable headless macOS workflow in `doc/mm2.md` — the injection sequence that reaches a London Blitz race, all `THESEUS_*` debug knobs (frame/flip/source/texture dumps, z-test toggle, key taps/clicks/holds, missing-address feedback), the `RUST_LOG` split between diagnostics (`debug`) and real problems (`warn`/`error`), and the known non-blocking noise list. Marked the corresponding ordered-backlog item complete. No code changes; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` remains green. Next blocker: none internal — retranslation needs the decrypted PE and the remaining warnings are missing game content.
- SelectObject noise iteration: the per-frame `SelectObject: unknown object`/`ignoring null select` warnings were identified last session as the game passing a non-GDI internal pointer from its UI text path — matching real Windows NULL failure, so they are benign. Demoted both to `debug!` (the caller read stays; it is one stack dword per call), keeping `unknown HDC` at `warn` since that would indicate a real anomaly. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi`, `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. A 60s `RUST_LOG=warn` London Blitz run is now clean: only the four expected `.CHK` cache misses remain (the files are absent; CreateFileA ENOENT logging is useful signal). Audited dynamic resolution too: `GetProcAddress`/`LoadLibraryA` warn on unsupported lookups and none fired; `mciSendCommandA` is a debug no-op for the intro movie; `SetEvent`/`WaitForMultipleObjects`/`timeSetEvent` are not in the snapshot's import table. Next blocker: none — all remaining output is missing game content.
- Translucent-overlay iteration: the large opaque navy rectangle that appeared mid-race (~384x384 centered at (128,48), pixel 0x08EB) was traced through the present chain — the navy lived in the D3D render target `0x7e2468` itself, written by `rasterize`, not by any `Blt`/`BltFast`/colorfill (memory watchpoints on scalar/byte/slice writes confirmed the only writer was `d3d7.rs` pixel writes). A per-pixel log line captured the culprit draw: `prim=6` (triangle fan) quad spanning (128,48)-(512,432), `tex=0`, `zenable=0`, diffuse `0x80101f5d` — vertex alpha `0x80`. The game alpha-blends a translucent panel (a UI/backdrop tint drawn every frame); the rasterizer had no blending and wrote it opaque. Implemented D3D7 alpha blending: `D3DRENDERSTATE_ALPHABLENDENABLE`/`SRCBLEND`/`DESTBLEND` are now tracked, a `blend_factor`/`blend_565` pair evaluates the standard D3DBLEND factors per 5/6/5 channel, and the pixel loop blends against the destination when enabled — source alpha is the interpolated vertex diffuse alpha (565 textures carry no alpha under MODULATE). Also added cheap diagnostics used during the hunt: `flip[N]` numbering, a `SetColorKey` debug line, a `Blt` colorfill debug line, a z-buffer dims line on `Clear`, and a `THESEUS_NO_CLEAR_SKIP` escape hatch for the mid-scene clear heuristic. `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests) and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass; a 110s scripted London Blitz run shows the panel correctly translucent (world visible through it, cyan nav arrow now blends too) with the race still progressing. Remaining artifact: horizontal banding/noise on thin distant geometry (fences, poles, gates) — likely missing mip selection or a texture-pitch issue — and the translucent panel's own content (text/bitmap) still does not draw, probably the missing `nodeGetBitmap` assets.
- Mipmap-chain iteration: every `CreateSurface` with `DDSD::MIPMAPCOUNT`+`DDSCAPS::MIPMAP` (`caps=0x403808`, ~690 in-race textures) now builds its sub-level chain — each level a `create_one_surface` at half the previous size, pushed onto the parent level's `attachments`, so the game's `GetAttachedSurface` walks succeed (was `nothing attached` for all of them; now ~3450 successful walks per run). The rasterizer picks a mip level per triangle by texel density — `0.5·log2(texel-space area / pixel-space area)` — and samples that level's pixels, skipping levels the game never locked (unfilled levels are left out of the chain). D3DTSS_MIPFILTER=18 set explicitly to D3DTFP_NONE(1) disables the chain. Result on a 100s London Blitz run: the sky's dusk gradient, distant building facades, the "Fleet Street" storefront sign, and the minimap street plan all render correctly now — they were previously point-sampling the base level. `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests) and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass. Remaining artifact: thin distant geometry (checkpoint gates, railings, poles) still aliases — likely needs per-pixel mip selection or a finer density estimate — and the translucent overlay panel's own content still does not draw.
- Texture-format/alpha iteration: the "banded" objects in race frames turned out to be alpha-bearing textures read as opaque RGB565 — the `CreateSurface` log showed `pf_flags=0x41` (DDPF_RGB|ALPHAPIXELS) with masks `0xf00/0xf0/0xf/0xf000` (A4R4G4B4) and `0x7c00/0x3e0/0x1f/0x8000` (A1R5G5B5) on ~40% of in-race textures, including the tree sprites whose canopies showed as rainbow noise. `Surface` now stores the requested `DDPIXELFORMAT` (falling back to the display 565 when unspecified), the rasterizer classifies it into 565/1555/4444, and `sample_texel` decodes each format to (565 color, 0-255 alpha). The pixel loop was reordered to D3D pipeline order — sample, then `D3DRENDERSTATE_ALPHATESTENABLE`/`ALPHAFUNC`/`ALPHAREF` rejection (which leaves depth untouched), then z-test/write, then blend — and the fragment alpha is now texel-alpha × interpolated diffuse alpha. Result: full-street frames render with proper building facades, dusk sky, checkpoint arrows, and a real minimap; the tree canopies still show residual noise in a few spots. `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests) and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass. Remaining artifacts: residual tree-canopy noise (possibly palettized 8bpp textures still read as 16bpp, or partial-mip alpha), the translucent overlay panel's missing content, and a white sky in some orientations.
- Rasterizer probe tooling: `THESEUS_PROBE=x,y` (render-target pixel coords) makes the D3D7 software rasterizer log every draw that writes that pixel — primitive/FVF, texture address/format/size/mip count, interpolated color and source alpha, and all relevant render/texture-stage state plus the triangle's vertex data — and dumps each distinct bound texture to `/tmp/probe_tex_<addr>.ppm`. Used to establish that the "banded" distant objects are plain 565 textures sampled at UVs far outside [0,1] (u≈4–28 — the game relies on D3DTADDRESS_WRAP tiling), and that real alpha-textured draws (A4R4G4B4 signs, `atest=1 afunc=GREATER aref=100`) now behave. As of the format/alpha/mip commits, a 90s headless London Blitz run produces a fully-formed race frame — yellow Beetle, London facades, dusk sky, checkpoint arrow, green timer, live minimap — with residual vertical-stripe aliasing confined to heavily-minified distant geometry (the checkpoint crowd/gate at the horizon). Later frames show a blank void where the blind accelerate input drove the car out of the course — the game loop and minimap stay alive, so it is a scripted-input artifact rather than a renderer failure.
- 150s stability run: a headless London Blitz race (menus → London → Blitz → checkpoints → accelerate) completed with zero crashes, zero unhandled/invalid-instruction faults, and zero invalid-memory accesses. The remaining stderr is content-level noise the game tolerates: missing `aud\aud22\*.22k` agesound files (the audio archive layout isn't fully served), missing `*_headlight*` medium/low LODs, absent optional `*.CHK` integrity files, three `nodeGetBitmap` misses for UI description bitmaps, a `london: room count mismatch` city-data check, `datParser::Read()` unrecognized tokens ('Ocean', 'Approach'), and the DMusic `ScanDirectory` finding no music files. The race loop stays alive and the minimap keeps updating even when the blind scripted input drives the car off-course into a blank void. With alpha-blend + mip-chain + texture-format/alpha-test fixes landed, the in-race frame is visually correct (yellow Beetle, London facades, dusk sky, checkpoint arrow, HUD timer, live minimap); the only residual render artifact is fine vertical-strip aliasing on the most heavily minified distant content (checkpoint gantry/crowd), which is plausibly authentic busy content rather than a bug.
- Horizon-banding classification: probing render-target pixel (410,190) with `THESEUS_PROBE` shows that strip is covered by dozens of overlapping quads bound to many distinct textures — building facades (565), the A4R4G4B4 "CROWN"/"ALLOW" signage surfaces, a 32x128 crowd/decal texture with real per-texel alpha (sa=17..187), and untextured colored billboards. The fine vertical stripes are those many subpixel quads' colors interleaving under point sampling — an inherent nearest-neighbor software-rasterizer artifact on the most heavily minified content (the checkpoint gantry + crowd), not a wrong-format, missing-mip, or missing-alpha bug. A bilinear/trilinear filter would soften it further, but the fix is cosmetic and confined to a small horizon strip; the game remains correct and playable-looking without it.
- Bilinear texture filtering: the game sets `D3DTSS_MAGFILTER`/`MINFILTER` = D3DTFG_LINEAR(2) (and `MIPFILTER`=ANISOTROPIC(3), which the mip path already honors), but the rasterizer always point-sampled. `sample_texel` is now split into `decode_texel` (per-channel 8-bit RGBA for 565/1555/4444) + `pack_565`, with a `sample_texel_linear` that four-taps the texel-centered footprint (offset −0.5, wrap addressing) and lerps each channel. The rasterizer computes the texel-space derivative `rho` for any textured pixel when mips exist or a linear filter is set, uses it for the mip level, then picks MINFILTER vs MAGFILTER by `rho > 1`. Result: the London Blitz frame is now essentially correct — smooth facades, a legible "Royal Jeweler" storefront, the checkpoint gantry/crowd rendered as a soft filtered wash instead of hard stripes, and a clean shaded Beetle. Cost: roughly 4x texture work per textured pixel, so the rasterizer is measurably slower (fewer flips over an 80s run). `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests) and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass.
- Host clippy iteration: `cargo clippy -p host -- -D warnings` failed with `manual_is_multiple_of` and `type_complexity` in `host/src/sdl.rs`. Replaced `frame % every == 0` and `phase % 2 == 0` with `is_multiple_of`, and introduced a `ClickInject` struct for the `OnceLock` click-injection state. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check host/src/sdl.rs`, `cargo clippy -p host -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (49 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Added a `valid` flag to the parsed `TIME` and made `timeSetEvent` reject unknown flag combinations by returning 0; valid combinations still create the single periodic timer. Removed the unused `stub!` import. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/winmm/time.rs`, `cargo test -p winapi` (50 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Removed redundant `'static` lifetimes from `VTABLES`/`VTABLE_ENTRIES` constants in `ddraw/mod.rs`, `dinput.rs`, and `dsound.rs`; added a `#![allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` crate attribute because the Win32/DirectX API signatures have fixed high arities; fixed `bitmap_format.rs` lints (`cast_abs_to_unsigned`, `bool_comparison`, `len_zero`, `into_iter_on_ref`); fixed `ddraw1.rs` lints (`needless_borrow`, `manual_is_multiple_of`, `field_reassign_with_default` in `EnumDisplayModes`). `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` the touched files, `cargo test -p winapi` (50 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. - WinAPI clippy green: fixed the remaining 28 mechanical `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings` diagnostics across the crate. Highlights: converted `Into<ABIReturn>` impls in `ddraw/types.rs`, `gdi32/mod.rs`, and `handle.rs` to `From<_>`; fixed `unnecessary_unwrap` in `d3d7.rs` mip enumeration; collapsed nested `if`/`if let` chains in `ole32.rs` and `kernel32/file.rs`; elided needless lifetimes in `kernel32/process.rs` and `kernel32/thread.rs`; replaced `map_or` with `is_none_or`, `split(|c| c == ' ')` with `split(' ')`, `std::iter::repeat(...).take(...)` with `std::iter::repeat_n`, and other one-liner lints in `kernel32/`, `user32/`, `gdi32/`, `winmm/`, and `ddraw/`; added crate-level `clippy::module_inception` and `clippy::upper_case_acronyms` allows plus `# Safety` docs for the two `init_vtables` functions. `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (50 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all passed. - DOS and `tc` clippy green: `cargo clippy -p tc -- -D warnings` was blocked by `dos` clippy diagnostics. Fixed `dos` lints: `write_all` for the stdout `write`, remove `let _ =` on unit-returning `push`, replace `resize` with `vec![0; ...]`, elide a needless lifetime, add a `ReadFileFn` type alias, fix a needless borrow, and return directly from `dump_com`. Added `#![allow(clippy::upper_case_acronyms)]` for the historical `PIT`/`VGA`/`PSP`/`MCB` names. Fixed `tc` lints: removed no-arg `format!()` calls in `codegen/math.rs`, `codegen/misc.rs`, and `codegen/mod.rs`; collapsed the empty-branch dispatcher in `gen_instr` into a short-circuit `&&` chain; fixed needless borrows and `let` chains in `gather/block.rs`, `gather/mod.rs`, and `lib.rs`; renamed `Report::to_html` to `into_html`. `cargo clippy -p dos -p tc -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p dos -p tc -p winapi`, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. - Shared-crate clippy verification and 8bpp surface audit: `cargo clippy -p runtime -p host -p exe -p logger -p dos -p tc -p winapi -- -D warnings` passed, confirming the shared compiler/runtime/winapi stack is clean. A 60-second `RUST_LOG=debug` headless run sampled every `CreateSurface` log line; all surfaces created during the race loop are 2bpp (16-bit RGB/alpha formats), with no 1bpp, 4bpp, or 8bpp palettized surfaces. This rules out the "palettized 8bpp textures" hypothesis for the residual tree-canopy noise — the noise is almost certainly the already-classified nearest-neighbor horizon aliasing or partial-mip alpha, not a format the rasterizer misreads. `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: the remaining render artifacts (white sky in some orientations, translucent overlay panel content) are likely missing assets or uncaptured D3D state rather than texture-format bugs; the next independent step is to inventory `D3DRENDERSTATE`/`D3DTSS` defaults the game still relies on, or document the missing `nodeGetBitmap`/audio assets as external prerequisites.
- Visual race-frame verification: a 120-second headless run with `THESEUS_FRAME_DUMP=/tmp/mm2_last.ppm` produced a stable in-race frame (converted to PNG with ImageMagick). The frame shows the yellow Beetle, London building facades, grass, a translucent overlay panel, the checkpoint arrow, and the 00:07:54 timer; no crash, invalid instruction, or invalid-memory access occurred in the 120-second run. The only visible artifact is the overlay panel lacking text, consistent with the missing `nodeGetBitmap` description bitmaps (`ulock_amvpcab`, `vpcab_desc`, `ama_rank_desc`) and the absence of the medium/low LOD assets reported in the run log. `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: add a `D3DRENDERSTATE` inventory diagnostic to find any state defaults the game relies on that the rasterizer does not yet consult, or document the missing audio/DirectMusic/checksum/LOD content as external prerequisites.
- D3D render-state/texture-stage inventory: added `HANDLED_RENDER_STATES`/`HANDLED_TEXTURE_STAGE_STATES` sets and one-time `log::warn!` diagnostics in `SetRenderState` and `SetTextureStageState`. A 60-second headless run reports the unhandled values actually used: `D3DRENDERSTATE_TEXTUREPERSPECTIVE(4)=1`, `D3DRENDERSTATE_SHADEMODE(9)=1` (flat), and `D3DRENDERSTATE_TEXTUREMAG(137)=1`; plus stage-0 `D3DTSS_COLOROP(1)=2`/`COLORARG1(2)=2`/`COLORARG2(3)=0`/`ALPHAOP(4)=2`/`ALPHAARG1(5)=2`/`ALPHAARG2(6)=0`/`ADDRESSU(13)=1`/`ADDRESSV(14)=1`. The texture-stage values match the rasterizer's existing `(TEXTURE * DIFFUSE) MODULATE` and wrap assumptions, so the overlay text is not missing due to color/alpha ops. `D3DRENDERSTATE_SHADEMODE=1` is a genuine gap (rasterizer always interpolates), but the race frame looks correct, so it is not currently blocking. `D3DRENDERSTATE_TEXTUREPERSPECTIVE` and `D3DRENDERSTATE_TEXTUREMAG` are legacy states superseded by the `SetTextureStageState` filter/address paths. `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (50 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: decide whether to implement `D3DRENDERSTATE_SHADEMODE` or document the remaining missing audio/checksum/DirectMusic/LOD assets as external prerequisites.
- D3D flat shading and linear fog: implemented `D3DRENDERSTATE_SHADEMODE` in the rasterizer. When `SHADEMODE=FLAT(1)`, the first vertex's diffuse color is used for the whole triangle instead of Gouraud interpolation. Implemented D3D7 linear vertex fog using `D3DRENDERSTATE_FOGVERTEXMODE=3`, `FOGSTART=220.0`, `FOGEND=320.0`, and `FOGCOLOR=0xffe1dcd7`; view-space depth is `1/w_sum` (interpolated rhw), so distant geometry blends into the light beige fog color. The 120-second race frame now shows a soft foggy horizon and correct building fade, with no crash or invalid-memory access. `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (50 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: add the missing texture-stage color/alpha-op and address states to `HANDLED` (their values are already default-equivalent), document the missing audio/checksum/LOD content, or continue with longer stability runs.
- Clean default-equivalent D3D state diagnostics: added the D3DTSS color/alpha/address states (1-6, 13, 14) and the legacy `D3DRENDERSTATE_TEXTUREPERSPECTIVE(4)`/`TEXTUREMAG(137)` to the handled sets. The headless run now emits no false-positive warnings for states whose values match the rasterizer's existing defaults; only genuinely unhandled states such as `AMBIENT(139)` will still warn. `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (50 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: document the remaining missing audio/checksum/DirectMusic/LOD content as external prerequisites, or run a longer stability test.
- Document external content prerequisites: added an `External prerequisites that cannot be filled from this repo` section to `doc/mm2.md` listing the absent `aud\aud22` sound bank, DirectMusic segments, `.CHK` checksum caches, medium/low LOD meshes, and `nodeGetBitmap` description bitmaps (`ulock_amvpcab`, `vpcab_desc`, `ama_rank_desc`). These explain the overlay text and audio warnings and are not code blockers. The only remaining blocking external prerequisite is a compatible decrypted `MIDTOWN2.ICD` PE image supplied through `MM2_INPUT`. `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings` and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: run a 240-second stability regression to confirm no new faults after the fog/flat-shading changes, or mark the current milestone as ready and wait for the decrypted PE prerequisite.
- 240-second stability regression: ran `timeout 240 ./target/fast/mm2 game` with the headless input script. The game loaded London, ran the Blitz race, and hit a building before the timer reached `03:03:61`; no crash, invalid instruction, or invalid-memory access occurred. The only D3D warning is the remaining `D3DRENDERSTATE_AMBIENT(139)` unhandled state. The captured frame shows the yellow Beetle in the city, fog on distant buildings, and the translucent overlay panel (still without text due to the missing `nodeGetBitmap` assets). `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: the remaining work is either cosmetic (tree canopy aliasing, overlay text) driven by external assets, or requires a decrypted PE to translate fresh code and discover any missing entry points.
- Process-environment iteration: `SetEnvironmentVariableA` returned success without storing anything and `GetEnvironmentStrings`/`GetEnvironmentVariableA` always saw an empty environment, so a translated program could never read back a variable it set. Added `State::env` (ordered, case-insensitive names per Windows semantics) and implemented `SetEnvironmentVariableA` (NULL value deletes, `=`/empty names fail with `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER`), `GetEnvironmentVariableA` (length-excluding-nul return, required-size-with-nul on short buffers, `ERROR_ENVVAR_NOT_FOUND`), `GetEnvironmentStrings`/`GetEnvironmentStringsW` (real `NAME=VALUE` snapshots allocated from the process heap), and `FreeEnvironmentStringsA/W` (actually frees the snapshot). `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (52 tests, including two new env round-trip tests) passed; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed; a 20-second headless run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with only the known missing-content warnings. Next blocker: `HeapReAlloc` still returns 0 for a function the game calls, plus `HeapDestroy` not removing heaps; then `user32` `SetFocus`/`EnableWindow`/`SetWindowTextA` state tracking.
- Heap lifecycle iteration: `HeapReAlloc` always returned 0 and `HeapDestroy` claimed success while leaving the heap registered — both are called by the game. `HeapReAlloc` now implements the real contract: null `lpMem` behaves like `HeapAlloc`, size 0 frees, shrinking stays in place, `HEAP_REALLOC_IN_PLACE_ONLY` fails rather than moving a block (the free-list allocator cannot extend a live block), and a growing realloc copies the old contents and zeroes only the grown tail under `HEAP_ZERO_MEMORY`. `HeapDestroy` removes the heap from `State::heaps` and warns on unknown handles. Added a `heap_realloc_grows_and_preserves` regression test. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi`, `cargo test -p winapi` (53 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed; a 20-second headless run was clean (7 warn/error lines, all known missing-content). Next blocker: `user32` `SetFocus`/`EnableWindow`/`SetWindowTextA` state tracking and `SetStdHandle`/`GetStdHandle` consistency.
- user32 window-state iteration: `SetFocus` (5 call sites in the snapshot) discarded the focus change and always returned null, `EnableWindow` never tracked the enable flag, and `SetWindowTextA` dropped the title. `State::focused` now tracks the keyboard-focus window — `SetFocus` validates the handle, posts `WM_KILLFOCUS`/`WM_SETFOCUS` (both added to the `WM` enum along with `ENABLE`/`SETTEXT`), and returns the previous handle; `ShowWindow`/`SetForegroundWindow`/`SetActiveWindow` set it, `DestroyWindow` clears it, and new `GetFocus` reports it. `Window` gained `title` and `enabled`: `SetWindowTextA` stores the string (new `GetWindowTextA` reads it back with truncation semantics), `EnableWindow` records the flag, posts `WM_ENABLE`, and returns the documented "was disabled" value, and new `IsWindowEnabled` reports it. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (53 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed; a 20-second headless run shows only the known missing-content warnings. Next blocker: `SetStdHandle`/`GetStdHandle` consistency and `WaitForMultipleObjects`.
- Std-handle and test-isolation iteration: `SetStdHandle` returned success without storing, so `GetStdHandle` could not report a redirected handle. `State::std_handles` now holds the three std handles (initialized to the console constants, which are `pub(crate)` and also feed the PEB `RTL_USER_PROCESS_PARAMETERS`), `GetStdHandle`/`SetStdHandle` read/write them with index validation, and `SetStdHandle` returns `BOOL`. While wiring this up, a flaky test-suite failure surfaced: the new tests each called `init_state`, which resets the shared `STATE` and can clobber another test's setup mid-assertion (3 failures observed once). Added `kernel32::ensure_test_state` (`#[cfg(test)]`, initializes only when the state is still empty), converted all test callers including `kernel32_is_loaded_at_process_initialization`, and merged the two environment tests into one so they cannot interleave on the env list. `cargo test -p winapi` passed three consecutive runs (52 tests). `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed; a 15-second headless run was clean (7 warn/error lines, all known missing-content). Next blocker: `WaitForMultipleObjects` still returns `WAIT_OBJECT_0` unconditionally, and `D3DRENDERSTATE_AMBIENT(139)` is the only unhandled D3D state warning in-race.
- `WaitForMultipleObjects` iteration: the stub always returned `WAIT_OBJECT_0`, so a program could not observe a timeout, an invalid handle, or which object fired. Implemented the real contract over the existing objects table: events expose their signaled state, mutexes are always signaled (single x86 thread), thread handles never signal (the one emulated thread does not exit); `bWaitAll=false` returns `WAIT_OBJECT_0` + lowest signaled index and consumes only that auto-reset event, `bWaitAll=true` requires every handle and consumes all of them, unknown handles return `WAIT_FAILED`, `nCount=0` fails, and finite timeouts poll with a 1ms sleep until the deadline. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (52 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: `D3DRENDERSTATE_AMBIENT(139)` unhandled-state warning in-race, then the msvcrt arity bugs (`srand`, `_controlfp`, `__getmainargs`, `_initterm` declare too few stdcall args and would corrupt ESP if called).
- `D3DRENDERSTATE_AMBIENT` iteration: the last unhandled render-state warning was `AMBIENT(139)`. Ambient only feeds the D3D lighting pipeline, which never runs for the pre-transformed, pre-lit `D3DFVF_XYZRHW` geometry the rasterizer draws (the only FVF the game submits), so the state is genuinely inert — it is already stored in `render_states` for `GetRenderState`. Added 139 to `HANDLED_RENDER_STATES` with that rationale. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (52 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. A 75-second headless London Blitz run (menus → London → race entry) reports only the four known `.CHK` checksum misses — zero unhandled-state or code warnings. Next blocker: the msvcrt stdcall arity bugs (`srand`, `_controlfp`, `__getmainargs`, `_initterm`, `__p__fmode`/`__p__commode`) that would corrupt ESP if the game ever calls them, then `MapWindowPoints` still ignores the tracked window origin.
- msvcrt cdecl iteration: the `dllexport` wrapper always pops `args.len()*4` (stdcall callee-cleanup), but the CRT exports are `__cdecl` — the caller cleans up — so any msvcrt function declared with its real parameters would double-pop the stack if a target imported it, and several were declared with *fewer* args than they take. Added `#[dllexport(cdecl)]` to the proc macro (wrapper pops only the return address) and marked every msvcrt export cdecl with the real signature: `srand(seed)` now seeds the MSVC LCG (`rand` also switched to the MSVC 214013/2531011 constants and `>>16 & 0x7fff` extraction), `_controlfp(new, mask)` translates the abstract `_MCW_*` control word to/from the x87 FPU control word (exception masks, rounding, precision, infinity; denormal-control stored separately), `__getmainargs` fills argc/argv/envp with the program name from the process command line, `__p__fmode`/`__p__commode` return stable guest-heap pointers (defaults `_O_TEXT` and 0), `_initterm(begin, end)` runs each CRT initializer through `ctx.indirect` + `call32_x86`, and `_XcptFilter`/`_except_handler3`/`exit`/`_exit`/`__set_app_type`/`__setusermatherr` got correct arities. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo check -p winapi`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (52 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed; a 15-second headless run was clean (7 warn/error lines). Next blocker: `MapWindowPoints` still ignores the tracked window origin; remaining stubs are all content-bound or unreferenced.
- Coordinate-mapping iteration: `MapWindowPoints` ignored the window's tracked screen position and always applied a zero delta, and `mouse_msg` filled `MSG.pt` (documented as screen coordinates) with client coordinates and a zero timestamp. `MapWindowPoints` now returns the window origin (`window.x`/`window.y`, with no frame chrome modeled) for the known HWND and `(0,0)` for the desktop/null, and `mouse_msg` adds the same origin to `MSG.pt` and stamps `time` via `host::host().time()` like `key_msg` already did. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (52 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed; a 20-second headless run with a scripted click was clean (7 warn/error lines). Next blocker: remaining `stub!` uses are all content-bound or unreferenced by the game; audit `user32` menu/accelerator stubs and `kernel32` `GetProfileIntW`/profile-string reads for any that could bite a different target.
- Private-profile iteration: `GetPrivateProfileStringW` returned `ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND` (2) as the character count, violating the contract — a caller would read 2 bytes of stale buffer as the "string". Implemented a real profile model: `read_ini` parses the .ini file (sections, case-insensitive names, `;`/`#` comments), `GetPrivateProfileIntW` does an atoi-style leading-integer coercion of the value or `nDefault`, and `GetPrivateProfileStringW` handles the null-`lpAppName`/`lpKeyName` enumeration forms (multi-string with double-NUL, `nSize-2` overflow contract) plus the single-value path that copies the default on a missing file/key. Added `read_wstr`/`write_wstr`/`write_wstr_multi`/`atoi` helpers and a regression test covering the default-copy and truncation contracts. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (53 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: audit remaining `stub!` uses actually referenced by the generated snapshot — most remaining stubs are unreferenced or content-bound.
- Stub-reference audit and 240-second regression: cross-referenced every remaining `stub!` use against the generated snapshot — only `SetCapture`, `CharPrevA`, and `SetCooperativeLevel` are referenced, and all three are already honest implementations (capture tracking, byte-step CharPrev for SBCS, host-managed cooperative level). The remaining stubs (`GetDesktopWindow`, `ValidateRect`, menu/accelerator/dialog loaders, `waveOutReset`/`waveOutClose`, `TranslateAcceleratorA`) are unreferenced by the game or correctly model absent functionality. Ran the full 240-second headless sequence (`THESEUS_INJECT_VKEY`/`INJECT_CLICK`/`INJECT_HOLD`) — the game navigated menus to London, entered the Blitz race, and produced a stable in-race frame (`/tmp/mm2race3.ppm`, 640x480, 13.5k unique colors) with zero crash, invalid-instruction, or invalid-memory errors and only the four known `.CHK` misses among warnings. `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (53 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. Session tally: 8 milestones this pass — kernel32 environment, HeapReAlloc/HeapDestroy, user32 focus/enabled/title state, SetStdHandle round-trip plus test-isolation fix, WaitForMultipleObjects, AMBIENT render state, msvcrt cdecl convention plus real signatures, MapWindowPoints/MSG.pt origins, private-profile INI contract, and this regression. Next blocker: none in code — fresh translation requires the external decrypted `MIDTOWN2.ICD` PE image (`MM2_INPUT`), and remaining warnings are all missing-content.
- Message-filter iteration: `GetMessageA`/`PeekMessageA` (both on the generated import path) asserted that the queue head matched the requested `hWnd`, which would panic the translated process as soon as a filtered read saw a different window's message at the head, and both ignored `wMsgFilterMin`/`wMsgFilterMax`. Added a shared `MsgFilter` implementing the documented selection — `NULL` sees every thread message, `(HWND)-1` sees only null-hwnd thread messages, a real handle sees only that window's messages, plus the min/max message range — with `GetMessage` returning -1 for a handle that is not the emulated window, `WM_QUIT` written into `lpMsg` before the 0 return, and the quit flag delivered regardless of filters once no matching posted message remains. Added two queue-level regression tests covering window/range filtering and quit delivery. `rustfmt --edition 2024 win32/winapi/src/user32/message.rs`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (55 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed; a 20-second headless menu run reached `Just before GameLoop: 3.0M` with only the known missing-content warnings. Next blocker: `DispatchMessageW` still discards the window-procedure return value, and host events arriving before window creation panic in `msg_from_message`.
- DispatchMessage iteration: `DispatchMessageW` unwrapped the registered window class and always returned 0 instead of the window procedure's result. It now validates the message read, resolves the wndproc only when the message targets the emulated window (thread and foreign-window messages return 0 like Windows), and propagates the wndproc's EAX return value. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (55 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: `msg_from_message` unwraps the window for any host event arriving before creation; `SetTimer` still cannot create a timer.
- Pre-window host-event iteration: `msg_from_message` unwrapped the emulated window, so any host event arriving before `CreateWindowEx` panicked the translated process, and host Quit filled wParam/lParam/time with placeholder zeros. The conversion now returns `Option`, records `WM_QUIT` as a null-hwnd thread message even before the window exists (with a real timestamp), and drops other host events while still applying their shared input-state update. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (55 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: `SetTimer` still cannot create a timer, and `waveOutReset`/`waveOutClose` never stop the emulated stream.
- SetTimer iteration: `SetTimer` always failed (`stub!(0)`), so a target could never arm a `WM_TIMER`. `MessageQueue` now tracks timers (per-`(hwnd,id)` replacement, clamped `uElapse`, allocated ids for null-hwnd timers) and synthesizes `WM_TIMER` at the lowest priority — only when no posted, quit, or paint message matches — with peek marking the message pending so due timers coalesce, and removal rescheduling the next firing. `KillTimer` removes real timers, `SetTimer` validates a non-null hWnd against the emulated window, `WaitMessage`/`GetMessage` poll briefly instead of blocking while timers exist, and `DispatchMessageW` routes `WM_TIMER` with a non-null lParam to the SetTimer `TIMERPROC` (`hwnd`, `WM_TIMER`, id, time). The millisecond clock is supplied by callers so the queue stays host-free; two new tests cover firing/coalescing/rescheduling and null-hwnd id allocation. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: `waveOutReset`/`waveOutClose` never stop the emulated stream; `SetWindowPos` ignores position/size changes.
- waveOut lifecycle iteration: `waveOutReset`/`waveOutClose` returned success while the feeder thread kept playing, and `waveOutWrite` panicked when no callback-style open existed (the receiver was dropped). The channel now carries a `WaveMsg` enum; the feeder drains `Reset` by clearing the SDL stream and returning every pending block marked WHDR_DONE with a WOM_DONE callback, exits when `waveOutClose` drops the sender (destroying the stream via a new `AudioStream::Drop`), and polls the channel while SDL's queue is full so control messages are never stuck behind a backlog. `waveOutReset`/`Close`/`Write`/`PrepareHeader`/`UnprepareHeader` validate the one emulated handle, `waveOutClose` reports `WAVERR_STILLPLAYING` while blocks are outstanding (tracked via a shared pending count), the feeder thread now runs for every callback style, `PrepareHeader`/`UnprepareHeader` maintain `WHDR::PREPARED`, and `AudioStream` gained `clear()`/`Drop` on SDL. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -p host -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. MM2 itself does not import waveOut (it uses DirectSound); this is shared-model work for other targets. Next blocker: `SetWindowPos` ignores position/size changes; `GetDesktopWindow`/`ValidateRect`/`SetMenu`/`MessageBoxW` remain stubs, all unreferenced by the snapshot.
- Window-geometry iteration: `SetWindowPos` returned `stub!(true)` while ignoring every change, `InvalidateRect` panicked on the legal null hWnd (whole-screen invalidate), and `GetClientRect` ignored its hWnd and panicked before the window existed. `SetWindowPos` now honors SWP_NOMOVE/SWP_NOSIZE, updates the tracked origin and size (resizing frees the stale pixel buffer and marks the window dirty), and posts WM_MOVE/WM_SIZE like Windows; `InvalidateRect` accepts null and rejects foreign handles; `GetClientRect` validates the handle like `GetWindowRect`. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: `GetDesktopWindow`/`ValidateRect`/`SetMenu`/`MessageBoxW` remain stubs, all unreferenced by the snapshot.
- LoadStringW iteration: `LoadStringW` asserted `cchBufferMax > 0`, panicked on a missing string resource, and copied the whole UTF-16 string into a `cchBufferMax`-sized buffer — an out-of-bounds panic for long strings. It now mirrors `LoadStringA` and the real contract: 0 for bad buffers or missing resources, truncation to `cchBufferMax - 1` units plus a nul, and a memory-bounds check. `find_string` also returns `None` on a malformed string block instead of unwrapping. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: `GetDesktopWindow`/`ValidateRect`/`SetMenu`/`MessageBoxW`/`CheckMenuItem` remain stubs, all unreferenced by the snapshot; `kernel32` `SetEvent` still asserts auto-reset-only.
- Event semantics iteration: `SetEvent` unwrapped the object table and asserted `!manual_reset`, so a bad handle or a manual-reset event panicked the translated process. `SetEvent` now validates the handle, wakes every waiter for manual-reset events (which stay signaled), and wakes one for auto-reset; `ResetEvent` clears the flag. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: remaining `stub!` uses (`GetDesktopWindow`, `ValidateRect`, `SetMenu`, `MessageBoxW`, `CheckMenuItem`, menu/dialog loaders, `TranslateAccelerator`) are all unreferenced by the snapshot or correctly model absent functionality; the generated-code path is clean.
- Stub-honesty iteration: several `stub!` returns were either the correct answer or actively misleading. `ValidateRect` now clears the window's dirty flag so a caller can actually retire `WM_PAINT`; `MessageBoxW` logs the text like `MessageBoxA` and returns `IDOK` instead of an invalid 0; `GetDesktopWindow` returns the model's null-desktop convention silently; `CheckMenuItem`/`SetMenu` return the no-menu-model answers; `IDirectDraw::SetDisplayMode` returns `DD::OK` after the resize it already performs. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed; a 30s headless run reached the menus with only the known missing-content warnings. Next blocker: menu/dialog loaders and `TranslateAccelerator` remain stubbed but unreferenced; the generated-code path has no remaining stubs or known panics.
- Paint-path iteration: `BeginPaint`, `EndPaint`, and `GetDC` unwrapped the window unconditionally, so a call before `CreateWindow` or with a foreign handle panicked; `BeginPaint` also unwrapped the window class and the `PAINTSTRUCT` write. All three now validate the handle against the emulated window and return a null `HDC`/`false` on bad input, `BeginPaint` releases the fresh DC when the `PAINTSTRUCT` write fails, and `fErase` still tracks whether the class registered a background brush (a `Brush` may legitimately carry no color). `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. Next blocker: remaining stubs (`LoadMenuW`, `LoadAcceleratorsW`, `DialogBoxIndirectParamW`, `DialogBoxParamW`, `EndDialog`, `TranslateAccelerator*`, `GetMenuItemRect`) are unreferenced by the snapshot; the on-path API surface has no remaining panics on plausible input.

No crashes, invalid instructions, or invalid-memory errors. Remaining stubs are all unreferenced by the snapshot (menu/dialog loaders, `TranslateAccelerator`, `GetMenuItemRect`); the on-path API surface has no remaining panics on plausible input. - Unnecessary-cast iteration: `cargo clippy -p mm2` reported `clippy::unnecessary_cast` for `mul` (`eax as u32 as u64`), `imul` (`eax as u32`), `bt` (`(eax) as u32`), and `call/jmp eax` (`ctx.indirect(eax as u32)`). Made the accumulator register in `mul`/`imul1` size-specific so no redundant `as u{size}` appears; made `bt` skip the cast when the bit-index register is already 32 bits and `In`/`Out` skip `as u{width}` for 32-bit operands; and skipped `as u32` in `indirect` for 32-bit register targets. `rustfmt --edition 2024 tc/src/codegen/math.rs tc/src/codegen/misc.rs tc/src/codegen/control_flow.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs`, `cargo clippy -p tc -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p tc` passed with 134 tests; a fresh `tc` translation of `game/Midtown2.exe` no longer contains `as u32 as u64` or `eax as u32`. - Mapping-overlap iteration: new `Mappings` regressions reproduced two real `insert_index` bugs. A fixed-address reservation that overlapped the previous mapping was silently inserted whenever the gap before the *next* mapping was large enough (`cargo test -p runtime mapping` failed: `reserve_fixed_mapping_overlapping_previous_panics` did not panic and `reserve_fixed_mapping_past_address_space_panics` panicked with a raw `attempt to add with overflow` at `mapping.addr + new_mapping.size` instead of the `no space` diagnostic). `insert_index` now computes the fixed reservation's end once with `checked_add` (overflow → `no space`), rejects `addr < prev_end` as an overlap, and keeps the gap-fit behavior for auto allocations. Four new tests cover gap placement, previous-mapping overlap, end-of-space overflow, and first-gap `alloc`. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check runtime/src/mapping.rs`, `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p runtime` (131 tests) all pass. - `read_str` robustness iteration: `Memory::read_str` (used by ~55 winapi `*A` call sites for guest paths/names) panicked on a missing NUL before end of memory and on any non-UTF-8 byte, even though CP-1252 high bytes are legal in ANSI strings. New tests in `runtime/src/memory.rs` reproduced both panics; `read_str` now logs an error with the caller and address and returns `""` on unterminated or non-UTF-8 input, matching the runtime's graceful-degradation convention. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check runtime/src/memory.rs`, `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p runtime` (134 tests) all pass. The only remaining `objects.get` unwraps are inside tests. `rustfmt --edition 2024` on the three files, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests) all pass. - user32 handle-validation iteration: several `user32/window.rs` paths panicked on plausible guest state — `MoveWindow` and `ReleaseDC` unwrapped `state.window` (panic if no window exists yet; `ReleaseDC` with a non-null HWND flushed unconditionally), `MapWindowPoints` unwrapped `Ptr::read`/`write` on the guest point array, `RegisterClassW` unwrapped `lpWndClass` and panicked on a non-brush `hbrBackground`, and `Window::flush` unwrapped `self.pixels` even when nothing had been drawn into the buffer. All now degrade to the documented failure value (`false`/`0`, skip the flush). `rustfmt --edition 2024` on window.rs, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (57 tests) all pass. - Heap-robustness iteration: `HeapAlloc`/`HeapSize`/`HeapFree` unwrapped `state.heaps.get(&hHeap)` so an unknown handle panicked; `Heap::free`/`Heap::size` computed `addr - 4` before bounds checks so `HeapFree(hHeap, 0, NULL)` underflowed; `Heap::alloc` panicked on OOM; `HeapReAlloc` trusted `lpMem`; and `GlobalAlloc` asserted `!GMEM_MOVEABLE` even though identity handles plus no-op `GlobalLock`/`GlobalUnlock` already satisfy moveable semantics. Added `Heap::try_alloc` (Option), made `Heap::free` return whether the block was accepted, guarded `size`/`free` with `addr < 4 || !range.contains(addr-4)` returning `u32::MAX`/`false`, wired the API entry points to the documented failures (NULL/FALSE/-1), and accepted `GMEM_MOVEABLE`. A new `heap_apis_degrade_on_bad_handles` test covers unknown-heap and bad-`lpMem` paths. `rustfmt --edition 2024` on both heap files, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (58 tests) all pass. This session's shared-code audit is complete: workspace clippy is green except the three documented external inputs, all package tests pass, and the stale MM2 snapshot still builds and runs a 100s headless London Blitz race with no internal crash. - waveOut guest-input iteration: `waveOutGetDevCapsA` asserted `cbwoc` and wrote `pwoc` unchecked, `waveOutOpen` unwrapped the `WAVEFORMATEX` read and wrote `phwo` without validation, `waveOutPrepareHeader`/`UnprepareHeader`/`Write` asserted `cbwh` and unwrapped the `WAVEHDR` reference, and the feeder thread unwrapped header reads — all host panics on hostile or simply bad guest input. Added `usable_range`/`guest_read`/`guest_mut` bounds helpers (null-page plus end-of-memory), `MMSYSERR_BADDEVICEID`/`INVALPARAM` returns, `WAVE_MAPPER` device acceptance, the documented `WAVERR_UNPREPARED` write rejection, and single-lock handle checks in `waveOutReset`/`waveOutWrite`; the feeder thread now drops unreadable blocks while keeping `pending` consistent. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (60 tests) pass. - mmio guest-pointer iteration: `mmioRead` sliced `ctx.memory[pch..]` unchecked, `mmioDescend`/`mmioAscend` read `MMCKINFO` through `Memory::read` without bounds checks, and `mmioGetInfo`/`mmioSetInfo`/`mmioAdvance` indexed `ctx.memory[lpmmioinfo..]` before the prefix parse, so any out-of-range guest pointer panicked the host. Added a shared `guest_fits` range check, failing `mmioRead` with `MMIO_FAILURE` when the destination cannot hold the read, validating `lpck`/`lpckParent` in descend/ascend, and routing every `MMIOINFO` access through `get`/`get_mut` so truncated buffers return `MMIOERR_CANNOTOPEN`. A new `mmio_calls_reject_out_of_range_guest_pointers` regression covers the bad-pointer paths. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (61 tests) pass. Both now write through `get_mut`, keep a byte/unit free so a truncated result stays NUL-terminated, and still return the full produced count. - ptr bounds iteration: `Ptr::read` and `Ptr::write` checked the null page but then sliced `memory[addr..][..size]`, so any guest pointer past the end of emulated memory panicked the host with a slice error. - msvcrt iteration: `__getmainargs` wrote its `argc`/`argv`/`envp` out-pointers after a `!= 0` check only, and `_initterm` walked `[begin, end)` reading each entry unchecked, so a bogus `end` could walk off the end of emulated memory and panic the host mid-boot. - D3D7 vertex buffer zeroing hardening iteration: `IDirect3D7::CreateVertexBuffer` allocated a vertex buffer and then zeroed it with `ctx.memory[data..][..data_len as usize].fill(0)`. If the allocated address or `data_len` pointed past emulated memory, the host panicked. Added a `get_mut` range check and return `DD::ERR_GENERIC` when the buffer cannot be zeroed. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (72 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass; a 15s headless smoke run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with no panic and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: the remaining direct `ctx.memory[...]` slices in `ddraw/d3d7.rs` light/material/execute copy paths, `dsound`/`winmm` host-side unwraps, or the next shared-code audit pass.
- ddraw7 surface DC scratch copy hardening iteration: `IDirectDrawSurface7::GetDC` allocated a scratch RGBA buffer for non-32bpp surfaces and copied the converted pixels with `ctx.memory[scratch..][..rgba.len()].copy_from_slice(&rgba)`. If the scratch allocation or `rgba` length extended past emulated memory, the host panicked. Replaced the unbounded slice with a `get_mut` range check and a chained `if let` so a missing range silently skips the copy (the surface will be all-black, which is a safer fallback than a panic). `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (72 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass; a 15s headless smoke run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with no panic and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; the `RALPH_AGENT_PASSES=12` budget for this session is now exhausted; next candidates in a future pass: the remaining direct `ctx.memory[...]` slices in `ddraw/d3d7.rs` `SetLight`/`GetLight`/execute paths, `dsound`/`winmm` host-side unwraps, and the next shared-code audit pass.
- d3d7 Clear hardening iteration: `IDirect3DDevice7::Clear` read the `lpRects` array with unchecked `ctx.memory.read` and the `fill` closure sliced `ctx.memory[at..][..len]` directly. Added a `guest_range`/`checked_mul` validation for the rect list, and rewrote the fill helper to range-check each scanline with `ctx.memory.bytes.get_mut` before invoking a `&mut [u8]` writer. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (72 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass; a 15s headless smoke run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with no panic and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: the remaining direct `ctx.memory[...]` slices in `ddraw/d3d7.rs` `SetLight`/`GetLight` and the `ddraw7` `ReleaseDC` scratch read.

Replaced the direct slice with `ctx.memory.bytes.get`/`get_mut` so the helpers return `DD::ERR_INVALIDPARAMS` instead of panicking if the range disappears. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (72 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass; a 15s headless smoke run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with no panic and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. - Mappings::try_alloc iteration: `Mappings::alloc` placed auto-sized mappings at the end of the table unconditionally, so `VirtualAlloc` and `HeapCreate` could return addresses past the end of emulated memory — every subsequent access panicked the host. - VirtualFree iteration: `VirtualFree` returned success unconditionally, so a guest releasing and re-allocating regions leaked address space until `try_alloc` failed. Added `Mappings::free`, which removes a dynamically created mapping by base address and refuses to release `section` mappings (the loaded image); `VirtualFree` now honors `MEM_RELEASE` (with the required `dwSize == 0`), no-ops `MEM_DECOMMIT` since all emulated memory is committed, and returns FALSE for unknown types and unmapped bases.
