The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: `gdi32/bitmap.rs` `Bitmap::parse(&ctx.memory[lpbmi..])` (SetDIBitsToDevice header pointer) and the `nls.rs` `ctx.memory[addr..]` readers in `read_string_type_a`/`read_string_type_w`/`read_multibyte`.
- Bitmap/nls bounds iteration: `SetDIBitsToDevice` sliced `ctx.memory[lpbmi..]` before parsing the `BITMAPINFO` header (panic on an out-of-range header pointer), and its `xSrc + w`/`xDest + w`-style checks were u32 adds that a huge start coordinate could wrap past; `StretchBlt` had the same wrap-prone checks and u32 row-offset math. The header read now uses `bytes.get` (returning 0 when absent) and all four rectangle checks widen to u64; row indexing is usize throughout. `Bitmap::stride` computed `width * bit_count` in u32 — a hostile `biWidth` produced a wrapped-tiny stride that let `read_pixels` index a too-short row — it now computes in u64 and saturates to `u32::MAX`, `pixels_len` uses usize math, `read_pixels` guards `x1 > x2` and computes `(y+1)*stride` and all row offsets in usize, and `BITMAPCOREHEADER::stride` no longer multiplies in u16. The `nls.rs` readers `read_string_type_a`/`read_string_type_w`/`read_multibyte` took `&ctx.memory[addr..]`, panicking on a pointer past emulated memory; they now use `bytes.get(addr..)` so `GetStringTypeA/W` and `MultiByteToWideChar` report failure. Added `read_pixels_handles_a_huge_declared_width` and `read_pixels_rejects_x1_past_x2` regressions. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests) pass. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: remaining `ctx.memory[..]` slices on internally-derived addresses (resource.rs section reads, ddraw blit row copies) or the next audit cluster.
- LoadLibrary/DLL-path iteration: `LoadLibraryA` sliced the on-disk PE with `&buf[rsrc_off..rsrc_end]`, which panics when a truncated or corrupt DLL declares `PointerToRawData` past the file end (`start > end`), and `rsrc_rva + rsrc_vsize` was an unchecked u32 add that could wrap into a too-small mapping while `loaded_modules` recorded a `resources` range past emulated memory. The raw-data read now uses `buf.get` with a saturated end (failing the load when the range is invalid), and `rsrc_rva + rsrc_vsize` goes through `checked_add`. `GetModuleFileNameA` sliced `ctx.memory[lpFilename..]` after checking only `nSize`, so a buffer pointer near the end of memory panicked; it now validates `copy + 1` bytes via `crate::ddraw::guest_range`. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/kernel32/dll.rs`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass; a 15s headless smoke run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with only the known missing-content warnings and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: continue the audit in `host`/`runtime` (`unsafe_clone` aliasing, `Context::indirect`/`call32_x86` on bad guest addresses) or pick up any new smoke-run finding.
- kernel32 string/time pointer iteration: `read_c_string`, `read_counted_a`, and `read_counted_w` (feeding `lstrlenA`/`lstrcpyA`/`lstrcatA`/`lstrcmpA`/`CompareStringA`/`CompareStringW`), `lstrcpyW`, and `lstrlenW` all sliced `ctx.memory[addr..]` directly, so a guest pointer past emulated memory panicked the host; they now go through `bytes.get` and return their documented failure values. `GetTimeZoneInformation` and `FileTimeToSystemTime` filled `lpTimeZoneInformation`/`lpSystemTime` without a range check — both now `get_mut` the bounded range, returning `TIME_ZONE_ID_INVALID`/`false` on a bad pointer. `GetCurrentDirectoryA` sliced `lpBuffer` after checking only `nBufferLength`; it now validates the `len + 1` range via `guest_range` and returns 0. `GetEnvironmentVariableA`'s `fits` check lacked a null-page guard, so a sub-0x1000 buffer address with a large `nSize` wrote into the null page; it now requires `lpBuffer.addr >= 0x1000`. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` on the five files, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests) pass. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: the `user32`/`dinput`/`dmusic` guest-pointer cluster (`misc.rs` `lpchText`, `window.rs` title write, `input.rs` key-name/state writes, `resource.rs` LoadString copies, `dinput.rs` caps/property/device-name buffers, `dmusic.rs` caps fill).

In dinput, `GetCapabilities`/`GetProperty`/`SetProperty`/`GetDeviceInfo` read `dwSize`-style fields through `Memory::read` (panic past end of memory) and added `pdiph + DIPROPDWORD_DWDATA` without an overflow check — all now use `Ptr` reads/writes and `checked_add`, returning `DIERR_INVALIDPARAM`; `GetDeviceData` computed `rgdod + i * cbObjectData` in u32 (a guest stride could wrap the write into a different address) and unwrapped `pdwInOut` — entry addresses are now computed in u64 and each write goes through `Ptr::write`. `dmusic::write_port_caps` read `*caps` unchecked; it now uses `Ptr::read`. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` on the five files, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass. All six `mixer*` exports trusted their struct pointers past the `< 0x1000` check: `mixerGetDevCapsA`/`mixerGetLineInfoA`/`mixerGetLineControlsA`/`mixerGetControlDetailsA`/`mixerSetControlDetails`/`mixerOpen` now validate `pmxcaps`/`pmxl`/`pmxlc`/`pmxcd`/`pamxctrl`/`phmx` via `guest_range` before `Memory::read`/`write`, including the `pamxctrl` control-buffer range. `GetCPInfo` wrote through an unchecked `lpCPInfo` (now `Ptr::write`), `LCMapStringA`/`LCMapStringW` wrote `lpDestStr` after only a `cchDest` size check (now `output_range_fits`), and `WideCharToMultiByte` read `lpDefaultChar` and wrote `lpUsedDefaultChar` unchecked (now `Ptr` access). `IDirectDrawPalette::GetCaps`/`GetEntries`, `IDirectDrawSurface::GetPixelFormat`, `IDirectDraw7::GetCaps` (`lpDDDriverCaps`/`lpDDEmulCaps`), `IDirectDrawSurface7::GetCaps`/`GetClipper`/`GetDC`/`GetPalette`/`GetPixelFormat`, and `IDirect3DVertexBuffer7::GetVertexBufferDesc` all took out-pointers that were only checked for null — each now validates the exact range it writes; `GetEntries` additionally clamps to `min(dwNumEntries, entries - dwBase)` before validating so `entries[dwBase..]` cannot slice past the palette, and `GetSystemPaletteEntries` validates `cEntries * 4` up front with a `wrapping_add` index. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` on the touched files, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 15s headless smoke run all pass — the run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with only the known missing-content warnings and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. - Thread-creation resource iteration: `kernel32::State::init_thread` `expect`ed both the TEB and stack `try_alloc` results, and `create_thread` `unwrap`ed `Builder::spawn`, so a guest that exhausted mappings (CreateThread spam, or a leaked mapping) or the host thread budget panicked the emulator. `init_thread` now returns `bool` (with `aligned_mut` on the TEB also handled as an `Option`), `create_thread` propagates `false` and drops the object-table handle on either failure, and `CreateThread` returns `HANDLE::null()`. The initial-thread call in `init_process` keeps its fatal semantics via `assert!`. `fs_base` was confirmed not writable by generated code (`gen_addr_offset` only reads it), so `teb()`/`teb_mut()`'s `unwrap` is not guest-reachable. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 15s headless smoke run all pass — `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M`, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: the `host`/`runtime` internal audit (e.g. `push32`/`cpu_loop` on a corrupted `esp`) or any new smoke-run finding.

- dmusic out-pointer iteration: the hand-rolled DirectMusic stubs were the largest remaining cluster of panicking `Memory::write` on guest-supplied out-pointers — `stub_out!` (u32/u64 variants), `query_interface!`, `Init_stub`, `PlaySegment_stub`, `GetTime_stub`, `PChannelInfo_stub`, `CreatePort_stub`, `AutoTransition_stub`, and the three `create` bodies all wrote `ppv`/`out` after only a `!= 0` check, so a non-null out-of-range pointer panicked the host. - guest-controlled panic sweep: audited every `unwrap`/`expect`/`assert!`/`panic!`/`unreachable!` outside test modules. Three sites could panic the host on guest input: (1) `user32::LoadImageA` asserted `is_intresource(name)`, `typ == BITMAP`, `fuLoad.is_empty()`, and `bitmap.width/height == cx/cy` — all now `log::warn!` + `HANDLE::null()` returns, and the size check now treats `cx`/`cy` of 0 as "natural size" per the real API (previously a zero cx would panic on a nonzero-size bitmap); (2) `gdi32::CreatePen` asserted `iStyle == 0` and `cWidth == 1` — now warns and degrades to a solid pen; (3) the `win32flags` macro's `FromABIParam::from_abi` did `from_bits(val).unwrap()`, panicking on any unknown guest flag bit across every flag-typed API parameter — now `from_bits_retain(val)` so callers see the unknown bits and can warn-and-ignore. Everything else was mutex/condvar `lock().unwrap()`, startup-fatal process setup, or internal invariants (blit `unreachable!`, TEB `aligned_ref`, `register_module`/`module_handle`). `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 15s headless smoke run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with no panic and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: a longer/varied smoke run to surface new runtime findings, then remaining non-memory robustness (integer overflow, arithmetic on guest sizes).
- driven-race smoke run: ran the documented injection sequence `RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 THESEUS_MISSING_ADDRS=out/mm2/missing.txt THESEUS_FRAME_DUMP=/tmp/mm2.ppm THESEUS_INJECT_AT_MS=5000 THESEUS_INJECT_VKEY=0x0d,0x0d,0x28,0x28,0x28,0x28,0x0d THESEUS_INJECT_CLICK="540,450;530,440" THESEUS_INJECT_CLICK_MS=15000 THESEUS_INJECT_CLICK_GAP=2000 THESEUS_INJECT_HOLD=0x26@45000 timeout 150 ./target/fast/mm2 game`. The game reached `SELECT DRIVER`, switched to London, started a Blitz race, loaded the level (city extents, roomcheck, waypoints, AIMAP Init all present), and ran through `Reset!!!` to `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 12.2M`, running until the 150s timeout (exit 124 is the expected kill). No panic, no invalid-memory fault, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`, and the 921KB frame dump at `/tmp/mm2.ppm` confirms a real frame was presented. Only the known tolerated warnings appeared (missing aud22 content, DirectMusic content absent, LOD warnings, london room-count mismatch). No code changes; the guest-pointer and panic sweeps held through a full race. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: audit arithmetic on guest sizes (integer overflow in `* count`/`+ offset` computations feeding allocations or slices), or take another sweep category.
- arithmetic/parser panic audit: checked guest-size subtraction, `addr + n`, `a * count`, and division across winapi. Verified guarded: `write_wstr`/`write_wstr_multi` (nSize gates + checked_add), string-copy `n-1`/`cchBufferMax-1` computations (all guard `<= 0`), `create_surface` (u64 product + 64 MiB cap), `sample_texel*` (zero-dimension early return), dsound `frame_bytes` (channels/bits clamped ≥1 in `from_wave`, `% size` under `size > 0`), rasterizer `w/area` (f32 division can't panic), `heap.rs` `addr - 4` (guarded by `addr < 4` + range check), `event.rs` `any.unwrap()` (guarded by `is_some`), `Bitmap::stride` (u64 saturate), `read_str`/`read_wstr`/`Ptr` (bounded). One real panic found: `bitmap_format::parse` sliced `&buf[header_size as usize..]` for BITMAPINFOHEADERs — a malformed file declaring `biSize` past the buffer end panicked; now `buf.get(..)` → degenerate bitmap, matching every other parse-failure path. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 15s headless smoke run reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with no panic and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. No `todo!`/`unimplemented!` in reachable winapi code; all `stub!` macros return fixed values gracefully. The guest-pointer, guest-panic, arithmetic, division, string-read, parser, resource-exhaustion, and non-winapi sweeps are now all complete with no remaining known unguarded path. `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (77 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 15s headless smoke run all pass. Added a `dialog_out_pointers_tolerate_bad_addresses` regression covering out-of-range and valid out-pointers. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (78 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. - TLS hardening iteration: `TlsGetValue`/`TlsSetValue` indexed `TEB.TlsSlots` with the raw guest `dwTlsIndex`, so any index ≥ TLS_MINIMUM_AVAILABLE (64) panicked the host, and `TlsAlloc` never reported exhaustion while `TlsFree` was missing entirely. `State::tls_allocated` is now a 64-bit slot bitmask: `TlsAlloc` hands out the lowest free index and returns `TLS_OUT_OF_INDEXES` once all 64 are held, `TlsGetValue`/`TlsSetValue` reject index ≥ 64 with `ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER` in `LastErrorValue`, and new `TlsFree` clears the allocation bit and zeroes the calling thread's slot per the documented contract. `tls_slots_round_trip_and_reject_bad_indices` covers alloc/set/get/free plus the bad-index paths. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (80 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. - D3D7 render-state default iteration: `GetRenderState` returned 0 for states the guest never set, while the rasterizer applied the real D3D7 defaults (CULLMODE=CCW, ZFUNC=LESSEQUAL, SRCBLEND=ONE, ALPHAFUNC=ALWAYS, SHADEMODE=GOURAUD, ...). A guest save/restore (`old = GetRenderState(X); SetRenderState(X, new); ...; SetRenderState(X, old)`) of an unset state therefore restored 0 — e.g. CULLMODE=0 silently disables culling, ZFUNC=0 selects NEVER. A shared `default_render_state` now holds the defaults the rasterizer draws with, and both `GetRenderState` and `rasterize` read through it, so save/restore round-trips consistently. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (81 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. The lock now splits a wraparound region across the two pointer pairs and rejects `dwOffset >= size` and `dwBytes > size` with `DSERR_INVALIDPARAM`, matching the documented contract. Two regressions cover the wrap (`0xe0 + 0x30` in a `0x100` buffer yields `0x20`+`0x10`) and the out-of-range failures. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p winapi` (85 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. - mmioRead hardening iteration: `winmm::mmioRead` sliced `ctx.memory[pch..]` even when the computed read length was zero, so a `cch == 0` call with an out-of-range `pch` panicked the host, and `mmioDescend` can leave `file.pos = body + 4` past EOF for a truncated container chunk, which underflowed the `end - file.pos` subtraction. The failure now logs and reports an empty queue so drain waits resolve instead of corrupting the accounting. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p host --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass. - GDI bottom-up subregion iteration: `StretchBlt`/`BitBlt` and `SetDIBitsToDevice` flipped a bottom-up source with `hSrc - y_src - 1`/`h - y_src - 1`, which is only right when `ySrc == 0` and the blit spans the whole bitmap — a `ySrc > 0` subregion read the wrong rows (or nothing, when the row index wrapped past `read_pixels`'s guard), and `SetDIBitsToDevice` also sliced only `h` rows of `lpvBits`, so a nonzero `ySrc` truncated the read. `StretchBlt` now reads `bmp_src.height - 1 - y_src` (GDI coordinates are top-down over the bitmap), and `SetDIBitsToDevice` reads `ySrc + h - 1 - y` (its `ySrc` is the lower-left corner in bottom-up coordinates) with the source range widened to `ySrc + h` rows. - Heap live-block tracking iteration: the recurring `ignoring double free` warnings during races all traced to `IDirectDrawSurface7::Release` freeing `surface.pixels` — blocks the free list already considered free. The root cause was that `FreeList::free` trusted the in-band 4-byte size header at `ptr-4`, which is guest-visible and corruptible: a live block whose header the guest overwrote produced an oversized or misplaced free node covering still-allocated memory, `try_alloc` then handed out that "free" space to surface pixel buffers, and `Release` warned. `FreeList` now keeps a `live` map of block headers (`BTreeMap<addr, size>`) written at `alloc` time; `free` validates against it, so interior, stale, foreign, and double frees return false without touching the free list, and the recorded size replaces the corruptible in-band read. `Heap::free` is `#[track_caller]` and logs the Rust call site on a rejected free; `HeapReAlloc` validates `lpMem` through the new `Heap::block_size` (the tracked payload size) so a bad pointer can no longer feed a garbage length to `copy_within`. Also fixed a latent `insert_index` bug: a freed block sorting above every existing free node was inserted at index 0 instead of the end, leaving the list unsorted and breaking later merge checks. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (102 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 100s headless London Blitz run all pass — `AIMAP.Init: Done!`, `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 12.2M`, zero heap warnings, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: `midiOutSetVolume` remains a stub the game tolerates, a different scripted menu path, or another audit category.
- SetFilePointer negative-seek iteration: `SetFilePointer` with `FILE_BEGIN` and a negative `lDistanceToMove`/`lpDistanceToMoveHigh` distance cast the i64 straight to `u64`, wrapping to a huge `SeekFrom::Start` offset — the file pointer moved to a bogus position (or read garbage low bits) instead of failing. A `distance < 0` check now returns `INVALID_SET_FILE_POINTER` with `ERROR_NEGATIVE_SEEK` (131) in `LastErrorValue`, leaving the file pointer where it was; `FILE_CURRENT`/`FILE_END` already fail through `File::seek`'s negative-result check. `set_file_pointer_rejects_a_negative_begin_position` covers the early return (a bogus handle still reaches it since the check runs before the lookup) and the recorded error. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (103 tests) all pass. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: a different scripted menu path, or another audit category.
- HeapSize live-block iteration: `HeapSize` still answered from the in-band 4-byte header at `ptr-4`, so a guest that scribbles over the header — the exact corruption behind the earlier free-list warnings — changed the reported size. It now consults `Heap::block_size` (the live-block map) and returns `(SIZE_T)-1` for anything that is not a live block; the header dword is still written at alloc time for guest code that reads `ptr-4` directly. `Heap::size` is gone as dead code. `heap_realloc_grows_and_preserves` now also asserts that `HeapSize` reports the requested payload even after the header is overwritten. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (103 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: a different scripted menu path, or another audit category.
- Input state iteration: two `GetAsyncKeyState`-visible gaps in `user32::input`. Auto-repeat keydowns re-armed the "pressed since last call" bit (`async_pressed`), so a held key looked freshly pressed on every repeat — repeats now only hold the key down, while a physical press still arms both the side-specific and generic VK. And mouse button transitions never touched the `keys[]` array, so `GetAsyncKeyState(VK_LBUTTON)`, `GetKeyState`, and `GetKeyboardState` all reported buttons as permanently up; `on_mouse` now mirrors left/right/middle transitions into VK_LBUTTON/RBUTTON/MBUTTON with the fresh-press bit. `autorepeat_does_not_rearm_the_async_press_bit` and `mouse_buttons_report_through_the_vk_state` cover both. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (105 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. - Display-settings iteration: `EnumDisplaySettingsA` wrote the DEVMODEA fields four bytes early — `dmBitsPerPel` landed in `dmFormName`/`dmLogPixels`, `dmPelsWidth` on `dmBitsPerPel`, `dmPelsHeight` on `dmPelsWidth`, and `dmDisplayFrequency` on `dmDisplayFlags` — while `ChangeDisplaySettingsA` already read the real offsets, so an enumerated mode could never round-trip. - CreateThread handle iteration: `CreateThread` returned `next_thread_id` as the handle, but `create_thread` registers the thread under `objects.add(Object::Thread)` — an independent counter — so the returned handle aliased whatever object happened to sit at that index (a file, event, mixer, …) and `WaitForSingleObject`/`GetThreadPriority`/`CloseHandle` on it acted on the wrong object or failed. `create_thread` now returns `Option<(HANDLE, u32)>` (the real object handle plus the thread id), `CreateThread` returns that handle and writes the `lpThreadId` out-pointer that was previously ignored, and the internal `winmm` spawn sites check `.is_none()`/ignore the result. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (106 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25s headless smoke run all pass with only the known external-content warnings. - Thread-wait iteration: thread handles could never be waited on — `WaitForSingleObject` always returned `WAIT_TIMEOUT` and `WaitForMultipleObjects` treated them as never-signaled, so a guest joining a finished thread would spin forever. `Object::Thread` now carries an `Arc<AtomicBool>` set when the thread's procedure returns; `WaitForSingleObject` polls it up to the timeout (`INFINITE` waits for real), and `WaitForMultipleObjects` counts a finished thread as signaled. The process's initial thread keeps an always-false flag so it is never signaled while the process runs. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (106 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 30s headless smoke run all pass with only the known external-content warnings. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: the `dplayx`/`dmusic` COM create paths that receive `ppv.addr` and may not validate it, or another audit category.

Widened the rect subtractions to i64 and returned `DD::OK` early when `copy_bytes`/`copy_rows` is zero. - LineTo unbounded-walk iteration: `gdi32::LineTo`'s Bresenham loop stepped from `dc.pos` to the raw guest `(x, y)`, so `LineTo(hdc, i32::MAX, i32::MAX)` would walk ~2^31 off-screen steps while discarding every pixel, and `(x - x0)` could overflow i32 in debug builds. Off-screen pixels are discarded anyway, so the walk's endpoints now clamp to just past the bitmap edges while `dc.pos` still records the real endpoint; the defensive in-bounds pixel-read `continue` that could spin without stepping became `break` (unreachable for simple bitmaps, but safer). Added a `line_to_clamps_far_offscreen_endpoints` regression. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/gdi32/dc.rs`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (108 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and `RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 THESEUS_MISSING_ADDRS=out/mm2/missing.txt timeout 15 ./target/fast/mm2 game` (reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M`, exit 124) all pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: remaining guest-controlled loop bounds (e.g. `fill_pixels`, `TextOutA` glyph loops) or another audit category.
- GDI fill/text loop-bound iteration: `fill_pixels` looped `y..y.saturating_add(height)` over raw guest i32s, so `Rectangle`/`PatBlt`-class calls with extreme coords would iterate billions of clipped-out pixels; `Rectangle`'s border walks had the same boundless ranges, and `TextOutA` ran the whole `c`-length string even after advancing past the bitmap's right edge. `fill_pixels` now clips to the bitmap before looping, the border walks clamp to `0..width`/`0..height`, and `TextOutA` breaks once `origin_x` exceeds the bitmap width (advancing `size.cx` only). Added a `rectangle_clips_extreme_coordinates_to_the_bitmap` regression. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/gdi32/dc.rs`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (109 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and `RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 THESEUS_MISSING_ADDRS=out/mm2/missing.txt timeout 15 ./target/fast/mm2 game` (reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M`, exit 124) all pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. - FXTRACT iteration: added `FPU::extract` and `tc` code generation for the x87 `FXTRACT` instruction. `extract` decomposes `ST(0)` into a signed integer exponent and a significand in `[1, 2)`, sets `ST(0)` to the exponent, and pushes the significand; zero/infinity/NaN inputs propagate their sign. `rustfmt --edition 2024 runtime/src/fpu.rs tc/src/codegen/fpu.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs`, `cargo test -p runtime -p tc` (141 + 140 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 15s headless smoke run (reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M`, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: the chained `memory[addr..][..len]` slice-indexing pattern, or another unimplemented x87/SSE path.
- Heap-allocation overflow iteration: `FreeList::alloc` computed the block size with an unchecked `size + 4`, so a guest `HeapAlloc`/`GlobalAlloc`/`HeapReAlloc` request at or above `u32::MAX - 3` wrapped the header-adjusted size to a small value and vended a tiny live block the guest believes is nearly 4 GiB; `HEAP_ZERO_MEMORY` then panicked the host on the `ctx.memory[addr..][..dwBytes]` fill, and non-zeroed requests silently corrupted memory. `alloc` now uses `checked_add` and fails the request. Added `alloc_rejects_sizes_that_overflow_the_header` and `heap_alloc_rejects_overflowing_sizes` regressions. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/heap.rs win32/winapi/src/kernel32/heap.rs`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p winapi` (111 tests) all pass. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: migrate the remaining non-test `ctx.memory[a..][..b]` chained-slice sites (`kernel32/env.rs`, `kernel32/strings.rs`, `kernel32/dll.rs`, `kernel32/misc.rs`, `user32/*`, `dinput.rs`, `dsound.rs`, `dmusic.rs`, `winmm/mmio.rs`, `ddraw*`, `msvcrt.rs`, `advapi32.rs`, `gdi32/dc.rs`, `dos/*`) to checked range access.
- Gather port-I/O iteration: `check_instr` in `tc/src/gather/block.rs` bailed on `In`/`Out`/`Ins*`/`Outs*` in non-DOS code, predating the Windows `port_in`/`port_out` model — it dropped real blocks (three loader jump targets: `00437f00`, `004c5254`, `00460072`) and would have regressed the MM2 image's real `in` paths on regeneration. Removed the rejection; `INVALID` still catches decode garbage. Also fixed a pre-existing `clippy::needless_range_loop` in `runtime/src/fpu.rs::bstp` and a `single_match` lint left by the removal. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check tc/src/gather/block.rs runtime/src/fpu.rs`, `cargo clippy -p runtime -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p runtime -p tc` (143 + 141 tests) pass. Re-running `cargo run -p tc -- --exe game/Midtown2.exe --out /tmp/mm2-tc-smoke2 --scan-memory --scan-immediates --scan-prologues` discovered the three port-I/O blocks (99380 -> 99384 blocks, still 93.8% coverage) and resolved `00460048 -> 00460072`; the remaining "unknown block" warnings trace to embedded .text data tables (`70 67 5b 00` = pointer 0x005b6770) whose bytes misdecode as `jo`, a benign low-confidence-scan artifact. `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` and a headless run to `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: `dos/src/dosapi.rs:256`/`dos/src/vga.rs:31` chained-slice sites (already guarded by explicit checks — verify invariants), or another audit category.
- FXTRACT exponent-sign iteration: `FPU::extract` (added in the Float80/FXTRACT commit) decomposed a negative input with `exp.copysign(val)`, negating the exponent — e.g. `-12.0` produced significand `-1.5` but exponent `-3.0` instead of `+3.0` (`-1.5 * 2^-3 = -1.5 != -12.0`); the existing test asserted the buggy value. Dropped the `copysign` so the exponent keeps its own sign, and corrected the `-12.0` expectation. `cargo test -p runtime` (143 tests) and `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` and a headless run to `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. Converted to `wrapping_sub`/`wrapping_add`, made the twelve `esp + N` argument reads in `win32/winapi/src/dmusic.rs` wrap, and hardened `dump_stack32`/`dump_memory16` (`machine.rs`) against `esp + i*4`/`i*2` overflow in the diagnostic path. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check` on the touched files, `cargo clippy -p runtime -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p runtime -p winapi` (143 + 111 tests) pass; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` and a headless run to `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. - _controlfp exception-mask iteration: `abstract_control`/`apply_control` in `win32/winapi/src/msvcrt.rs` copied the x87 exception-mask bits verbatim, but the abstract `_EM_*` layout differs — bits 0-4 are x87 {PM=5, UM=4, OM=3, ZM=2, IM=0} and `_EM_DENORMAL` (bit 19) is x87 DM (bit 1). Added `em_to_abstract`/`em_to_x87` permutation helpers and two regression tests (a `_EM_ZERODIVIDE`-only mask now flips only x87 ZM; full `_MCW_EM` masks/unmasks all six). `cargo test -p winapi` (113 tests) and `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` and a headless run to `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. - Far-branch-16-in-flat iteration: `gather` enqueued `IP::Seg(selector, ofs)` for `callf`/`jmpf ptr16:16` even in flat modules, so `to_addr` produced `selector*16+ofs` — a garbage low-memory decode that ended as "unknown block". `codegen` resolved the same wrong IP. Both now use `IP::Flat(offset)` when the module is not segment-addressed, and `FarBranch32` in a segmented module enqueues `IP::Seg` instead of treating the offset as flat. `cargo test -p tc` (141), `cargo clippy -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a headless run to `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. - Float-to-int CVT iteration: `cvtsd2si`, `cvttsd2si`, `cvtss2si`, `cvttss2si`, `cvtps2dq`, `cvttps2dq`, `cvtpd2dq`, `cvttpd2dq`, and `cvtps2pi`/`cvttps2pi` in `runtime/src/ops/xmm.rs` used Rust `as` casts and `round()` — wrong twice: `round()` is half-away-from-zero rather than the MXCSR-default round-to-nearest-even, and `as` maps NaN to 0 and saturates, where a masked x86 returns the integer-indefinite 0x8000_0000. Added `cvt_f64_to_i32`/`cvt_f32_to_i32` helpers implementing both behaviors and a regression test covering ties-even, truncation, NaN, infinity, and out-of-range. `cargo test -p runtime` (144) and `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` and a headless run to `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. Added `FPU::to_int(val, truncate, bits)` honoring the RC field (or truncation for FISTTP) and returning 0x8000../0x8000_0000/0x8000_0000_0000_0000 for invalid inputs; `tc/src/codegen/fpu.rs` now emits it, and the `codegen_handles_fisttp` expectation was updated. `truncate` stays for the stale generated output. - MINPS/MAXPS semantics iteration: `minps`/`maxps`/`minss`/`maxss` in `runtime/src/ops/xmm.rs` returned the non-NaN operand, but x86 defines the family as `SRC1 < SRC2 ? SRC1 : SRC2` — a NaN in the *second* source must propagate (the old code returned src1 instead), and equal operands (including the -0.0/+0.0 tie) must return src2 (Rust's `min`/`max` pick a side and `-0.0` wins incorrectly). All four helpers now implement the `a < b ? a : b` / `a > b ? a : b` rule directly, and the misleading "preserve the non-NaN operand" comment in `tc/src/codegen/xmm.rs` was corrected. Added `min_max_return_the_second_source_on_nan_or_tie` regression covering ordered lanes, NaN in either operand (src2 wins verbatim, including its NaN payload), the signed-zero tie, and the scalar lane-merge forms. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check`, `cargo clippy -p runtime -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p runtime` (146 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and `RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 THESEUS_MISSING_ADDRS=out/mm2/missing.txt timeout 15 ./target/fast/mm2 game` (reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M`, exit 124) all pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: the missing double-precision SSE2 ops (`Sqrtsd`/`Sqrtpd`/`Minpd`/`Maxpd`/`Minsd`/`Maxsd`) still bail translation via `not implemented`, or another audit category.

Added runtime helpers `sqrtpd`/`sqrtsd` (f64 sqrt per qword lane, NaN on negative, high-qword merge for the scalar form) and `minpd`/`maxpd`/`minsd`/`maxsd` (same `SRC1 < SRC2 ? SRC1 : SRC2` rule as the single-precision family), and wired the mnemonics into the existing `Sqrtps`, `Minps`, and `Addsd` codegen arms. Regressions `codegen_handles_sse2_double_minmax_and_sqrt` (six encodings incl. memory operands) and `sse2_double_minmax_and_sqrt_follow_single_precision_rules` (lane-wise sqrt, NaN propagation, signed-zero tie, scalar merge) pin the behavior. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p runtime -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p runtime -p tc` (147 + 142 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and `RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 timeout 15 ./target/fast/mm2 game` (reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M`, exit 124) all pass with no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. Codegen wires `Shufpd` into the `Shufps` arm via `instr_name`, `Unpckhpd`/`Unpcklpd` into the unpack arm, and `Movlpd`/`Movhpd` into the `Movlps`/`Movhps` arm, whose store-side qword pick now matches `movlps`/`movlpd`. The `codegen_handles_sse1_shuffles` and `codegen_handles_sse1_64bit_moves` tests gained the seven new encodings (loads, stores, and register forms), and `pd_unpack_shuffle_and_qword_moves` pins the lane semantics. `rustfmt --edition 2024`, `cargo clippy -p runtime -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p runtime -p tc` (148 + 142 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. - PSIGN operand-direction iteration: `psignb`/`psignw`/`psignd` (MMX) and `psignb_xmm`/`psignw_xmm`/`psignd_xmm` (SSSE3) in `runtime/src/ops/{mmx,xmm}.rs` matched on the *destination* lane's sign and copied/negated the *source* — the opposite of x86, where each destination lane is negated, zeroed, or kept according to the sign of the corresponding *source* lane. All six helpers now select on `src` and apply to `dest` (`wrapping_neg` keeps i8/i16/i32::MIN correct). New `psign_applies_the_source_sign_to_the_destination` (MMX widths) and `psign_xmm_applies_the_source_sign_to_the_destination` (XMM widths) pin the direction, including the negated-minimum wrap. - FINCSTP/FDECSTP TOP-wrap iteration: `FPU::inc_top`/`dec_top` in `runtime/src/fpu.rs` treated `st_top` as a bounded stack pointer, so FINCSTP from TOP=7 landed on `8` (the "all popped" sentinel — sticking the stack where `pop` no-ops) and FDECSTP from TOP=0 raised the exception stub instead of wrapping to 7. On real hardware TOP is a 3-bit mod-8 ring, so both now wrap: `inc_top` uses `(st_top + 1) % 8` (8 aliases TOP 0, so empty → 1) and `dec_top` uses `(st_top + 7) % 8` (0 and 8 → 7). Register contents and tags stay put, matching the spec that the pointer moves without freeing or marking registers. `fincstp_moves_top_without_changing_tags` now expects the 7→0 wrap, and `fincstp_fdecstp_wrap_top_mod_eight` covers the empty-sentinel alias and wrap in both directions. `cargo test -p runtime` (151), `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` pass. The private decrypted PE remains unavailable; next blocker: audit `winapi` surfaces or a scripted menu path.

- `Mappings::try_alloc` alignment fix: the allocator placed a new mapping at the raw `prev_end`, so an unaligned reservation (the 4-byte IAT at 0x1000) skewed every following base off a page boundary — e.g. `HeapCreate`/thread-stack/DirectSound allocations would land at 0x1004 rather than a page-aligned address. Windows guarantees page-aligned bases for VirtualAlloc-family allocations. `try_alloc` now aligns the candidate start up to the next page boundary in both the gap scan and the tail placement. Verified by `cargo test -p runtime` (153 tests, including a new regression test asserting an alloc after a 4-byte reservation lands at 0x2000), `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit (winapi/host surfaces) or another scripted path.
- Heap payload alignment fix (`winapi/src/heap.rs`): `FreeList::alloc` carried a `TODO: align` — payloads sat at `block + 4`, so every HeapAlloc result was only 4-byte aligned. Real HeapAlloc returns 8-byte-aligned memory on 32-bit Windows; guests using tagged pointers or assuming alignment on heap data could misbehave. `FreeList::new` now offsets the first block so its header sits at 4 mod 8 (payload at an 8-byte boundary), and `alloc` pads each block size to a multiple of 8 so the alignment holds for every later block. `HeapSize` consequently reports the padded usable size (e.g. an 8-byte request reports 12), which is permitted Win32 behavior. Verified by `cargo test -p winapi` (114 tests, updated the realloc/merge tests and added an alignment regression test), `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a headless scripted run (`THESEUS_INJECT_VKEY=0x0d,0x0d,0x28,0x28,0x28,0x28,0x28,0x0d`) that reached the lobby join path and `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.0M` with no panic or missing addresses. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or another scripted path.

- TEB stack-bounds fix (`winapi/src/kernel32/thread.rs`): `init_thread` allocated the thread stack but never wrote `NT_TIB.StackBase`/`StackLimit`, so a guest reading `fs:[0x04]`/`fs:[0x08]` saw zeros. - Heap-test isolation fix: `kernel32::heap`'s three tests all inserted `Heap::new(0x100_000, ..)` into the shared `STATE.heaps` map, so parallel tests evicted one another's live-block table — the mechanism behind the previously observed `heap_realloc_grows_and_preserves` flake. Each test now uses a distinct heap base (0x100_000/0x200_000/0x300_000) and a comment records the invariant. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check win32/winapi/src/kernel32/heap.rs`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and three consecutive `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` runs pass (120 tests each, zero failures). Both now zero the full 28/14-byte environment image via `memory.write_bytes` before writing fields, matching `fxsave`'s already-zeroed 512-byte image. `rustfmt --edition 2024 runtime/src/fpu.rs`, `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo test -p runtime` (153 tests) pass. Next blocker: continue the audit (other winapi surfaces, scripted paths) or the optional decrypted ICD.
- Heap range-overflow hardening: `Heap::range` and `FreeNode::range` computed `addr + size` with plain u32 addition, so a heap or free node whose claimed range wraps the 32-bit space would panic in debug builds or alias low addresses in release. All real callers are bounded by `Mappings::try_alloc`'s limit today, so this is latent hardening; both now saturate the end at `u32::MAX`. `rustfmt --edition 2024 win32/winapi/src/heap.rs`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (120 tests) pass. Audited `winmm/wave.rs` and `dsound.rs` (Release already refcounts shared duplicate data, Lock validates wrap ranges) and `heap.rs` `FreeList` merge/alignment — no other defects found. Next blocker: continue the audit or another scripted path.
- winmm timer wraparound hardening: `timeSetEvent` computed `next: host().time() + uDelay` and `winmm_main` rescheduled periodic timers with `now + t.period`; a guest-controlled `uDelay` (or a periodic timer straddling the ~49-day u32 ms wrap) would panic in debug or wrap `next` into the past, and since `winmm_main` compares `next <= now` directly, a wrapped `next` fires immediately rather than far in the future. Both now use `saturating_add`. `rustfmt --edition 2024 win32/winapi/src/winmm/{time,mod}.rs`, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (120 tests) pass. Audited `kernel32/time.rs` (QPC/QPF/GetTickCount/Sleep all correct), `user32/message.rs` (filter/timer/paint queues correct), `user32/input.rs` (key side-mapping, async-press and DI event buffers correct), all COM vtables (every named entry implemented), and `kernel32/file.rs` — no other defects found. - d3d7 draw-path bounds audit: `rasterize` collected the implicit index stream `(0..dwVertexCount)` before validating the vertex range, so a bogus count could force a proportional host-side Vec allocation; the non-indexed branch now guest_range-checks `dwVertexCount * vsize` first (the max-index check still bounds the indexed path). The z-buffer access assumed the attached surface matched the render target (`zstride = rt_width * 2`), so a mis-sized z-buffer would read/write outside its own allocation into adjacent heap memory; the rasterizer now records the attached surface's width/height, uses its pitch, and bounds per-pixel depth access to its extent. `DrawPrimitiveVB`/`DrawIndexedPrimitiveVB` computed `addr + dwStartVertex * vertex_size(fvf)` with plain u32 math and `CreateVertexBuffer` computed `data + data_len` likewise; both can wrap on guest-controlled counts and are now saturating/checked. - POINT/RECT arithmetic wrap iteration: `POINT::{add,sub,mul,div}`, `RECT::{add,size}`, and `user32::OffsetRect`'s `+=` used plain i32 arithmetic on guest-controlled coordinates (guest rect/point fields come straight from emulated memory via `OffsetRect`, `MapWindowPoints`, `GetWindowRect`, and the MoveWindow/SetWindowPos-fed window origin), so an extreme `dx`/`dy` or coordinate pair panicked the host in overflow-checked builds and silently diverged elsewhere. All now use `wrapping_*` (matching the real API's unchecked C arithmetic) except `div`, which uses `checked_div` and yields 0 on a zero divisor or the `INT_MIN / -1` overflow. `rustfmt --edition 2024` on the three files, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (121 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 20-second headless smoke run (`RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 THESEUS_MISSING_ADDRS=out/mm2/missing.txt timeout 20 ./target/fast/mm2 game`) reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.1M` with only the known external-content warnings and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. - `MoveWindow` hWnd/dimension iteration: `user32::MoveWindow` ignored its `_hWnd` argument entirely, so a call with a wrong or null handle still moved and resized the emulated window instead of failing; it also cast `nWidth`/`nHeight` straight to u32, so a negative size wrapped to a huge value that `Window::resize` clamped up to `MAX_WINDOW_DIM` rather than the documented 0. - RegisterClass atom-counter iteration: `State::register_class` incremented `next_class_atom` with plain `atom + 1`, so a guest registering classes in a loop reached `u16::MAX + 1` — a debug-build panic, and in release a wrap to atom 0 which reads as RegisterClass failure. The counter now wraps back to `0xC000` (the start of the registered-class atom range) instead of overflowing. The audit alongside covered `kernel32/resource.rs` `find_resource` (checked_add + `bytes.get`, already hardened), `kernel32/state.rs`, `handle.rs`, `locked_state.rs`, and the `msvfw32`/`imm32`/`vtuneapi`/`ebueula` stub modules — all clean; `Handles::reserve`'s `next + 1` and `next_object_handle` are u32 counters needing ~4 billion objects to wrap, and `next_timer_id` already uses `wrapping_add`. `rustfmt --edition 2024` on both files, `cargo clippy -p winapi --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 cargo test -p winapi` (121 tests), and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. The codegen now emits `reg.wrapping_mul({scale}u{bitness})` and the parenthesization for the first term is no longer needed. Added `codegen_uses_wrapping_mul_for_scaled_index_addresses` covering `mov eax, [ebx+ecx*4+0x12345678]`. - Bit-string memory-address overflow hardening: `tc/src/codegen/misc.rs` generated `{addr}.wrapping_add(((bit as i32 >> {shift}) * {bytes}i32) as u32)` for `BT`/`BTS`/`BTR`/`BTC` with a register bit index. The `as i32` made the shift arithmetic and the multiplication signed, so a bit index with the high bit set produced a negative offset that wrapped to the wrong address, and the `*` could panic an overflow-checked build. The expression now uses `(bit >> {shift}).wrapping_mul({bytes}u32)`, keeping the bit index unsigned and wrapping. Updated `codegen_uses_signed_offsets_for_memory_bit_indices` to `codegen_uses_unsigned_wrapped_offsets_for_memory_bit_indices` and verified `out/mm2/src/generated/` contains no `as i32 >>` bit-address patterns.
