Milestones covered: (1) San Francisco BLITZ, (2) San Francisco CIRCUIT, (3) San Francisco CHECKPOINT, (4) RACES-screen city-switch controls, (5) San Francisco BLITZ with forced city switch, (6) London BLITZ with forced city switch, (7) full-workspace verification, (8) 120-second San Francisco BLITZ soak with mixed inputs, (9) 120-second London BLITZ soak with mixed inputs, (10) clean menu-quit, (11) this final wrap. The RACES-screen city switch is now solved: the up chevron at (625,215) forces San Francisco and the down chevron at (625,229) forces London, so all race types in both cities are reachable from any saved profile city. Remaining blockers are external/optional: private decrypted ICD/PE input, real DirectPlay service providers, and missing original audio/LOD/UI-bitmap content.
|- 12th pass: worktree and missing-address audit: `git status --short` returns no untracked or modified tracked files, `out/mm2/missing.txt` does not exist, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` is a no-op pass. This consumed the final `RALPH_AGENT_PASSES` for this session. No new actionable shared-code or runtime blocker was discovered; the remaining work is external to the repository.
- MASKMOVDQU operand-order fix: `codegen_xmm` read operand 2 as the source data and operand 1 as the mask, but per the Intel SDM (`66 0F F7 /r`, `MASKMOVDQU xmm1, xmm2`) operand 1 (ModRM:reg) is the source data and operand 2 (ModRM:r/m) is the mask — the data and mask were swapped. Verified iced's operand list with a temporary decode probe (`maskmovdqu xmm0,xmm1` → op0=MemorySegEDI, op1=XMM0 data, op2=XMM1 mask) and corrected the code plus the existing regression's expectation and comment. The MMX `maskmovq` arm already had the correct op1=data/op2=mask order. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check tc/src/codegen/xmm.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs`, `cargo test -p tc codegen_handles_maskmovdqu` (1 test), and `cargo clippy -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings` all pass. Baseline before this pass: `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` no-op green, 25-second headless smoke reached the lobby (`Heap MemUsed: 3.1M`, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`). Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit (remaining `todo!` operand-kind arms in `tc/src/codegen`) or a scripted path.
- MMX `paddq`/`psubq`/`paddusw`/`psubusw` coverage: the MMX/SSE2 mnemonic guard in `tc/src/codegen/mmx.rs` listed all four, but no match arm lowered the MMX forms and `runtime/src/ops/mmx.rs` had no helpers — an MMX `paddq`/`psubq`/`paddusw`/`psubusw` would have bailed at translation. Added the four helpers (wrapping qword add/sub, saturating unsigned word add/sub), added the mnemonics to the shared binary-op arm, and extended `codegen_handles_packed_add_sub` with the `0F D4`/`0F DD`/`0F FB`/`0F D9` encodings. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check runtime/src/ops/mmx.rs tc/src/codegen/mmx.rs tc/src/codegen/mod.rs`, `cargo test -p runtime -p tc` (160 runtime + 147 tc tests), `cargo clippy -p runtime -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- `memory_kind_base` destination-register fix: the `MemoryESDI`/`MemoryESEDI`/`MemoryESRDI` op kinds (the implicit ES:DI/EDI/RDI destinations of `stos`/`scas`/`ins`-style instructions) were grouped with the `MemorySeg*` SI source kinds and returned `si`/`esi`/`rsi`. They now return `di`/`edi`/`rdi`, matching the `MemorySegDI`/`MemorySegEDI`/`MemorySegRDI` base registers. The bug was latent — string instructions go through dedicated `ctx.{movs,stos,scas,ins}*()` helpers — but any `get_op`/`gen_addr` on such an operand (e.g. an SSE2 `movsd`/`cmpsd` misdetected via `op0_kind()`) would have computed the address off ESI. Added `memory_kind_base_maps_implicit_registers` covering the SI/ES and DI/EDI/RDI split. `rustfmt --edition 2024 --check tc/src/codegen/mod.rs`, `cargo test -p tc --lib memory_kind_base` (1 test), and `cargo clippy -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings` all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- Dead `Div` arm removed from `codegen_misc`: `tc/src/codegen/misc.rs` had `Div => self.todo(instr_name(instr))` which would emit `todo!("div")` into generated code, but `codegen_math` already lowers `Div` fully (unsigned `div` with #DE trap on zero divisor or quotient overflow, per operand size) and runs earlier in `gen_instr`'s dispatch order, so the misc arm was unreachable dead code. `cargo check -p tc` and `cargo test -p tc --lib` (148 tests) pass; `rustfmt --edition 2024` clean. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- INT1/INT3 dispatch through the DOS IVT: `codegen_misc` emitted `unhandled_interrupt(0x1/0x3)` unconditionally, so a real-mode `int1`/`int3` could not reach the guest's interrupt handler the way `int N` and `into` already do. Both forms now emit `return dos::int(ctx, next_ip, vector);` for DOS modules and keep `unhandled_interrupt` for flat code; added `codegen_dispatches_dos_int1_int3` covering `F1` and `CC`. Also deleted the now-unused `CodeGen::todo` helper, which had no callers after the dead `Div` arm was removed — there is no remaining path that emits `todo!` into generated code. `cargo test -p tc --lib int1_int3`, `cargo clippy -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- CPUID leaf 1 feature bits now match implemented coverage: EDX reported only FPU|TSC|CX8|MMX, but the machine implements CMOVcc, FXSAVE/FXRSTOR, SSE, and SSE2, so guests feature-detecting them would take needlessly slow paths. EDX now also sets CMOV (15), FXSR (24), SSE (25), and SSE2 (26); the existing test expectation was updated. `cargo test -p runtime --lib` (160 tests), `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (lobby reached, `Heap MemUsed: 3.1M`, exit 0, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- LOOP/LOOPE/LOOPNE counter width now follows the address-size attribute: the runtime helpers picked CX vs ECX from `cpu.real_mode`, but the real distinction is the instruction's address-size attribute — `67 e2` in 32-bit code must count CX, and `67 e2` in 16-bit code must count ECX. The iced `Code` suffix (`*_CX` vs `*_ECX`) encodes this, so codegen now emits `loop_cx`/`loope_cx`/`loopne_cx` for the `*_CX` forms; the plain helpers are unconditional ECX. Added `codegen_picks_the_loop_counter_from_the_address_size` covering `e2`/`67 e2`/`67 e1`/`67 e0` in both code widths, and the runtime CX tests were moved to the new `*_cx` helpers. `cargo test -p tc --lib` (150), `cargo test -p runtime --lib` (160), `cargo clippy -p runtime -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, and `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- String ops now follow the address-size attribute rather than `real_mode`: `rep`, the (E)SI/(E)DI index advance, and the effective address all keyed off `cpu.real_mode`, so a `67`-prefixed string op in flat code would still count ECX and use full ESI/EDI, and a `67` prefix in 16-bit code wrongly kept 16-bit addressing. iced encodes the attribute in the implicit operand kinds (`MemorySegSI`/`MemorySegDI`/`MemoryESDI` are the 16-bit forms), so `codegen_string` now emits `rep16` plus `*_16` helpers (`movsb_16`, `stosd_16`, `cmpsw_16`, ...) when those kinds appear; the plain helpers are unconditional 32-bit addressing. `rep`/`rep16` split the ECX/CX count, `advance_index` takes `addr16` instead of `real_mode`, and a new `str_addr` truncates flat-mode 16-bit addresses to the low word while real mode still uses seg:ofs. Added `addr16_string_ops_advance_only_the_low_index_word` and `rep16`/`lodsb_16` runtime tests, and extended `codegen_routes_string_and_sse_movsd` with `67 a5` and `67 f3 a4` cases. Regenerating MM2 produced an unchanged snapshot (no 67-prefixed string ops in the image). `cargo test -p runtime -p tc` (161+150), `cargo clippy -p runtime -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (lobby, exit 0, no missing.txt) all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- XLAT honors the address-size attribute and segment overrides: `ctx.xlat()` picked BX vs EBX from `real_mode` and always read DS, so `67 d7` used the wrong base and `es:`/`fs:` overrides were ignored. Codegen now resolves the full address — `memory_base()` gives BX vs EBX (the 16-bit form wraps the BX+AL sum to 16 bits), `memory_segment()` drives `segofs` for segmented modules and `fs_base` for `fs:` in flat code — and the runtime helper just loads `ctx.xlat(addr)`. Added `codegen_handles_xlat_address_sizes` covering `d7`, `67 d7`, and `64 d7`. MM2's three `xlatb` sites regenerated to `ebx + al` form. `cargo test -p tc` (151), `cargo test -p runtime` (161), clippy, `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (lobby, exit 0, no missing.txt) all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- PUSHA/POPA/ENTER/LEAVE operand width now follows the operand-size attribute instead of `real_mode`: iced gives distinct `Pusha`/`Pushad` and `Popa`/`Popad` mnemonics, and `Leave`/`Enter` distinguish `Leavew`/`Leaved` and `Enterw_imm16_imm8`/`Enterd_imm16_imm8` by code. The runtime gained explicit `pusha`/`popa`/`enter16`/`leave16` helpers (the push16/push32 calls inside still resolve SP vs ESP from the stack-address attribute, which `real_mode` legitimately proxies), and codegen emits the matching helper per form — `66 60` in flat code now pushes 16-bit registers and `66 c9`/`66 c8` use BP/SP and word pushes. The existing real-mode tests moved to the explicit 16-bit names, and `codegen_handles_salc_rdtscp_icebp_and_16bit_pusha` now covers `66`-prefixed and `enter`/`leave` forms. `cargo test -p runtime -p tc` (161+151), clippy, regenerate + `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (lobby, exit 0, no missing.txt) all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path.
- Session wrap (compiler/runtime audit, continuation): this pass fixed the remaining address-size/operand-size conflation class — `real_mode` had been used to pick the counter and index width for `loop`/`loope`/`loopne`, all REP string ops, `xlat`, and the register/push width of `pusha`/`popa`/`enter`/`leave`, when each of those follows the instruction's `67`/`66` prefix attribute (or the iced code/mnemonic that encodes it) regardless of the module's code width. Also removed the last `todo!`-emitting codegen path (the dead `Div` arm and the now-unused `CodeGen::todo` helper), routed `int1`/`int3` through the DOS IVT, and extended CPUID leaf 1 to report CMOV/FXSR/SSE/SSE2. MM2 regenerated, rebuilt, and smoke-tested clean after each milestone; no `missing.txt`. Remaining external blockers are unchanged: no private decrypted ICD, no DirectPlay providers, no original audio/LOD/UI bitmaps, no WinPin/WinMD/BLASTER inputs. `cargo fmt --all` cannot run on `out/winpin` (generated module absent locally) — per-file rustfmt was used instead. Worktree is clean.
|- `runtime::fpu::bstp` out-of-bounds logging: `bstp` used `let _ = memory.try_write(addr, bytes);`, so an out-of-bounds or null-page destination produced no error log and the store silently dropped. It now uses `memory.write_bytes(addr, &bytes);` so the existing `Memory` logging reports bad addresses, matching the behavior of `fsave`/`fxsave` and other FPU stores. Added `bstp_out_of_bounds_does_not_panic` to verify the FPU still pops on a bad address. `rustfmt --edition 2024 runtime/src/fpu.rs`, `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p runtime` (162 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (`Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.1M`, exit 124, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit (`runtime`/`winapi` guest-pointer or FPU store validation) or a scripted menu/race path.

A fresh grep for `let _ = .*\.write\(` in `win32/winapi/src` returns zero matches; the only remaining `let _ = .*try_write` site in `runtime` was the `bstp` store fixed in the previous milestone. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted menu/race path.
|- `runtime::fpu::bld` out-of-bounds logging: `bld` used `memory.try_read::<[u8; 10]>(addr)` and silently pushed `0.0` on an out-of-bounds read. It now uses `memory.read::<[u8; 10]>(addr)`, which reports the bad address through the existing `Memory` logging and still decodes to zero for the caller. The existing `fbld_handles_negative_and_out_of_range` test continues to pass. `rustfmt --edition 2024 runtime/src/fpu.rs`, `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p runtime` (162 tests), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (`Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.1M`, exit 124, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. - `exe::IMAGE_SCN::align` no-flag underflow fix: `align()` computed `1 << (value - 1)` where `value` is the `IMAGE_SCN_ALIGN_*` field — a section with no alignment flag (value 0, meaning "default alignment" per the PE spec) underflows to `1 << 0xffffffff`, panicking in debug builds and producing `1 << 31` in release. It now returns 1 (byte alignment = no constraint) when no flag is set. The helper has no current callers — `characteristics()`/`align()`/`as_range` are latent API — so this is a shared-crate correctness fix, not a live mm2 path. New `section_align_without_flag_returns_default` test covers the flagless default and the encoded 1/2/4/8-byte values. `rustfmt --edition 2024 exe/src/file.rs exe/src/lib.rs`, `cargo clippy -p exe --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p exe` (9 tests), and a 25-second headless smoke (`Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.1M`, exit 124, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. - `sar` CF past the operand width: `ops/shift.rs::sar` returned `CF = false` for masked counts `y > operand_width` (e.g. `sar al, 9..31` on u8, `sar ax, 17..31` on u16), but every bit shifted out past the width is a sign-fill bit — hardware reports CF = the original sign. The else branch now returns `x.high_bit().is_one()`; the `y == bits` boundary was already correct via `x.shr(y - 1)` = MSB. `shl`/`shr` in the same range correctly stay CF = 0 (zero fill), and `sar`'s result/SF already saturate to all-sign. New `sar_past_the_operand_width_reports_the_sign_in_cf` test sweeps counts 9-31 on signed/unsigned u8 and u16 operands plus the `count == width` boundary. `rustfmt --edition 2024 runtime/src/ops/shift.rs`, `cargo test -p runtime` (163 tests), `cargo clippy -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (`RUST_LOG=warn THESEUS_HEADLESS=1 THESEUS_MISSING_ADDRS=out/mm2/missing.txt timeout 25 ./target/fast/mm2 game`) reached `Heap MemUsed (Just before GameLoop): 3.1M` with exit 124 and no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit (`ops/xmm.rs` lane ops, `tc` decoder prefix edge cases) or another scripted path.
- `gen_addr_offset` 16-bit flat addressing: a `67`-prefixed memory operand in flat 32-bit code made iced report 16-bit base/index registers (`BX`/`BP`/`SI`/`DI`) or a disp16-only form, and codegen emitted those `u16` `get_*` terms straight into the address expression — `u16.wrapping_add(u32)`/`read::<u32>(u16)` type errors that would break the generated build for any module containing such an operand, and the mod-0x10000 wrap of the effective address was not modeled. The flat path now detects 16-bit addressing (16-bit base/index register, base+index absent with `memory_displ_size() == 2`, or the implicit `MemorySegSI`/`MemorySegDI`/`MemoryESDI` string operands), widens each register term to u32, and masks the effective address with `& 0xffff`; `fs_base` stays outside the mask since it is part of the linear address. iced already reports displacements sign-extended to the address size (disp8 -> `0xfffe` under 67h), so masking the sum is exact. New `codegen_masks_16_bit_flat_addresses` covers `[bx+si]`, `[disp16]`, `[bp-2]`, `fs:[bx+si]`, and an unchanged 32-bit `[eax-2]`. `rustfmt --edition 2024 tc/src/codegen/mod.rs`, `cargo clippy -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test -p tc` (154 tests), `out/mm2/translate.sh` (99,384 blocks, 93.8% coverage, unchanged warning set), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (lobby, `Heap MemUsed: 3.1M`, exit 124, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit (`tc` operand-size prefix edge cases, remaining untested codegen paths) or a scripted path.
- `segofs32` for 32-bit addressing in 16-bit modules: the symmetric `67` case — a segmented module decoding a 67h-prefixed operand reports 32-bit base/index registers or a disp32-only form, which produced u32 expressions fed to `segofs(u16, u16)` and `u16` displacement literals too narrow for disp32. `instr_uses_16bit_addressing` now decides per operand (16-bit base/index register, disp16-only, or the implicit `MemorySegSI`/`MemorySegDI`/`MemoryESDI` kinds), `gen_addr_offset` widens terms and literals to u32 whenever the operand's address size differs from the module bitness, and `gen_addr` emits `segofs32` (new `runtime` helper keeping the full u32 offset) for 32-bit addressing in segmented modules. The segmented `xlat` path likewise picks `segofs32` when `memory_base` is EBX instead of truncating `off` with `as u16`. `codegen_masks_16_bit_flat_addresses` now also covers segmented `[eax+2]`/`[disp32]` (segofs32) and confirms ordinary `[si]+disp8` still uses `segofs`. `cargo test -p tc` (154 tests), `cargo test -p runtime` (163), `cargo clippy -p tc -p runtime --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `out/mm2/translate.sh` + `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (lobby, exit 124, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. - `Surface::dump` stride guard: the headless frame-dump path checked `last.len() >= height * stride` but then read `width * 4` bytes per row, so a `set_pixels` call with `stride < width * 4` would panic on the slice index. No current caller does that (all pass `width * 4`), but the debug facility now rejects narrow strides up front (`last_stride < width.saturating_mul(4)`), which also covers the `stride == 0` nothing-presented case. `rustfmt --edition 2024 host/src/sdl.rs`, `cargo clippy -p host --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (lobby, `Heap MemUsed: 3.0M`, exit 124, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. - Stale switch-index bound fix (`tc/src/gather/block.rs`): `BlockDecoder` recorded `cmp reg, N`/`and reg, mask` bounds in `index_bounds` but never invalidated them, so a register rewrite between the bound and a `jmp [reg*4+table]` dispatch made `scan_jump_table` read a fixed entry count and enqueue table-adjacent garbage as high-confidence code. `clears_index_bound` now drops the bound whenever a non-read-only instruction writes the register's op0 (sub-register writes included); over-clearing only loses the hint and falls back to stop-at-first-invalid scanning. New tests `index_bound_survives_to_the_switch_dispatch` and `index_bound_is_dropped_when_the_register_is_rewritten` gather a synthetic `cmp eax,7`/`jmp [eax*4+0x1800]` switch whose table has a valid entry past an invalid slot — reached only under the stale bound. `rustfmt --edition 2024 tc/src/gather/block.rs`, `cargo test -p tc` (156 tests), and `cargo clippy -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings` pass; `out/mm2/translate.sh` regenerated a byte-identical snapshot (`cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` no-op) and a 25-second headless smoke reached the lobby (`Heap MemUsed: 3.0M`, exit 124, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`). - RCL/RCR overflow-flag iteration: a new `rcl_rcr_multi_bit_counts_leave_of_undefined` regression reproduced `rcl`/`rcr` writing OF for every count while the SDM defines it only for a masked count of 1 (and the file's own rol/ror already gate on `masked == 1`). For RCR the SDM computes OF *before* the rotate loop as MSB(original operand) XOR pre-rotate CF; the old code used the ROR formula (MSB XOR MSB-1 of the result) unconditionally - equivalent only at count 1. Gated both on the 5-bit masked count (`y == 1`), computed RCR's OF from the pre-rotate operand/CF, and updated the two count=3 test expectations (`CF OF` -> `CF`). `rustfmt --edition 2024 runtime/src/ops/shift.rs && cargo test -p runtime` passed with 164 tests; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` passed. - Session baseline + switch-index bound hardening: worktree clean; `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2` no-op; 25-second headless smoke reached the lobby (`Heap MemUsed: 3.1M`, saved profile `City = London`), exit 0, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`. Audited `winapi/src/dinput.rs` and `winapi/src/dplayx.rs` (never previously covered): all guest pointers go through `guest_range`/`Ptr`, device refcounting cannot underflow (entry removed at 0), `GetDeviceData`/`SetDataFormat` stride math uses `checked_mul`/`checked_add`, and the DirectPlay vtable stubs return the correct provider-less DPERR codes - no defects. Found one gap in last session's stale switch-index bound fix: `clears_index_bound` only cleared a bound when the instruction's op0 was a written register, missing second-operand writes (`xchg`, `xadd`) and implicit writes (`mul`/`div`/`cdq`/`cwd` -> edx:eax, `popad`, `cpuid`, `rdtsc`, `loop`/`scas`/`stos`/`xlat`). Replaced it with `BlockDecoder::clear_index_bounds` driven by `iced_x86::InstructionInfoFactory` (default `instr_info` feature): every used register whose access can write (`Write`/`CondWrite`/`ReadWrite`/`ReadCondWrite`) drops its bound, keyed by `full_register()` like `is_index_bound`. New tests cover an unrelated-register write preserving the bound (`mov ecx,0`), a second-operand write (`xchg ecx,ebx`), and an implicit write (`cdq` on a `cmp edx,7` bound). `rustfmt --edition 2024 tc/src/gather/block.rs`, `cargo test -p tc` (159 tests), `cargo clippy -p tc --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `out/mm2/translate.sh` (byte-identical snapshot: 99,384 blocks, 93.8%), `cargo build --profile fast -p mm2`, and a 25-second headless smoke (lobby, `Heap MemUsed: 3.1M`, exit 124, no `out/mm2/missing.txt`) all pass. Next blocker: continue the shared-code audit or a scripted path (idle CHECKPOINT race-over attempt).
